lib/features/recording_studio/recording_studio_screen.dart — findings: 8
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:601-625 — `_resolveRecordingPath` performs synchronous filesystem I/O (`File(...).exists()`, `dir.existsSync()`, `File(resolvedPath).lengthSync()` at 670) on the UI isolate inside the existence scan and play path — jank/stutter on first paint with hundreds of takes — batch the scan off the build path and use async `exists()`/`length()` variants (the code already notes this intent at 601-603 but `lengthSync` at 670 and `existsSync` at 635 remain sync).
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:212-216 — `setState` called from an async continuation that may run after `dispose()` if the widget is removed mid-scan; the `mounted` guard at 205 protects the early-return branch but the `setState` at 212 is reached only when `mounted` was true at 205, so this is actually guarded — however `_scanFileExistence` is invoked from `_memoEntries` (line 84) which runs inside `build()` (line 104), launching un-awaited async work with no error path; an exception in `_resolveRecordingPath` (e.g. `getApplicationDocumentsDirectory` throwing on desktop) is swallowed silently and `_fileResolved` stays stale — wrap the async body in try/catch and log via `DebugLogService`.
- [medium] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:670 — `File(resolvedPath).lengthSync()` throws `FileSystemException` if the file was deleted between `exists()` and the stat (TOCTOU), and `_playRecording` has no try/catch around lines 651-684, so the throw escapes `_playRecording`'s caller (`onTap` at 373-377) and crashes the tap handler — wrap the stat in try/catch or use `File(...).length()` async with error handling.
- [low] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:196-198 — `_scannedKey` is built by joining recording ids with `'|'`; a recording id containing `'|'` (possible if ids are user-supplied or cache-prefixed) could collide with a different entry list, skipping a needed rescan — use a separator-free key (e.g. hash of the joined list or `ListEquality` compare).
- [low] lib/features/recording_studio/recordings_browser_screen.dart:616-621 — `firstWhere` with `orElse: () => ''` on `_cachedRecordingPaths` result: if the cache dir listing is empty the fallback returns `''` and `cachePath.isNotEmpty` is false, which is correct, but the predicate `path.contains(recording.scriptLineId)` can match an unrelated cache file whose path merely contains the line id substring — verify cache filenames are exactly the line id or tighten the match.
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_history_screen.dart:99-102 — `avgScore` divides by `sessions.length` after an `isEmpty` ternary that returns `0.0`, but the fold at 101 sums `averageMatchScore` over all sessions including any where the field is negative/NaN from upstream data; if any `averageMatchScore` is NaN the displayed 'Avg Score' percent becomes 'NaN%' — clamp or validate the field before folding (assumption: model field can be NaN; not visible in this batch).
- [low] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_history_screen.dart:274-280 — `_formatDate` uses `DateTime.now()` and `now.difference(dt)`; a session `startedAt` in the future (clock skew) yields a negative diff, `diff.inDays` is negative, falls through to `'${dt.month}/${dt.day}'` which is fine, but `diff.inDays == 0` on a session started 23h ago vs. a session started 1 minute ago both show 'Today' — acceptable, but the branch `if (diff.inDays < 7)` also catches negative values, printing e.g. '-3 days ago' for future-dated sessions — guard `diff.inDays >= 0` before the `< 7` branch.

- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3296-3322 — `_scrollToCurrentLine` runs inside a post-frame callback that reads `ref` and `_currentLineKey` after async gaps; if the widget unmounted between scheduling and the callback, `mounted` guard exists but `_scrollController.hasClients`/`_currentLineKey.currentContext` can still be stale — consequence: rare crash or no-op scroll after dispose — smallest safe fix: capture `mounted` and controller state before scheduling and bail early inside the callback (already partially done; add explicit `if (!mounted) return` before `addPostFrameCallback`).
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:226-236 — `_playerSub` subscription set in `initState` but never cancelled in `dispose` (only `_playerSub?.cancel()` at 606 exists — actually it IS cancelled; re-check) — consequence: none — smallest safe fix: none (withdrawn; see coverage).
- [high] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:242-383 — `_initAudio` performs many awaits (`SharedPreferences.getInstance`, `VoiceConfigService.getLocale`, `_loadProgressCheckpoint`) and then calls `ref.read`/`setState`/`ScaffoldMessenger.of(context)` after async gaps; `mounted` guards exist at 262/276/294/329 but the `_initSttDeferred` path at 381 and `_showResumeSnackBar` at 363 run after further awaits without a fresh `mounted` check — consequence: use of `context`/`ref` after unmount can throw "Using ref after dispose" or crash on missing ScaffoldMessenger — smallest safe fix: re-check `mounted` immediately before each post-await `ref`/`context` use (lines 363, 381).
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:119,1721-1722 — `_deferredProcess` timer fires `_processCurrentLine` guarded only by `mounted`; `_processingLine` flag is reset inside a `Future.delayed` that is not cancelled on dispose — consequence: after dispose the delayed closure still runs and touches `ref` (via `_processCurrentLine`) if `mounted` were true in a re-mounted widget; in a disposed state `mounted` is false so it returns, but the timer itself is not cancelled in `dispose` (line 590 cancels `_deferredProcess` — actually it does) — consequence: none — smallest safe fix: none (withdrawn).
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:501-510 — `ref.listenManual` subscription `_progressSub` is closed in `dispose` (588) but the listener callback mutates `_lastLineIndex`/`_persistKey` and schedules `_progressSaveTimer` from inside a provider listener that can fire after dispose if the provider changes between `dispose` start and `close()` — consequence: late timer persists progress with a stale key after unmount — smallest safe fix: close `_progressSub` before cancelling `_progressSaveTimer` (already ordered correctly) and add `mounted` check inside the listener callback.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:579 — `didChangeAppLifecycleState` calls `_persistProgress(_lastLineIndex)` which awaits `SharedPreferences.getInstance()` and uses `_persistKey` cached at setup; if `_persistKey` is null (never set because `_startProgressAutosave` was not reached due to an early return in `_initAudio`), `_persistProgress` silently returns and the checkpoint is lost — consequence: force-quit during init drops the actor back to line 0 — smallest safe fix: fall back to `_currentProgressKey()` when `_persistKey` is null, or log the miss.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:526-536 — `_persistProgress` writes `'$idx|${DateTime.now().millisecondsSinceEpoch}'` but `_loadProgressCheckpoint` (479-494) parses `parts.first` as index and `parts[1]` as millis; if `idx` itself contains a `|` (impossible for int) — consequence: none — smallest safe fix: none (withdrawn).
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:493 — `_loadProgressCheckpoint` returns null when `idx >= lineCount`, silently discarding a valid checkpoint after a script edit that shortened the scene; combined with `_clearProgressCheckpoint` never being called in that path, the stale key remains on disk and is re-read every entry — consequence: repeated wasted reads and a stale entry that never expires until 14 days — smallest safe fix: `await prefs.remove(key)` in the `idx >= lineCount` branch too.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:143-148 — `_maxListenFor` computes word count from `line.text` including stage directions; `_confirmDespiteNoise` (2427-2431) strips stage directions before the same computation — consequence: a line that is mostly stage directions gets an inflated ceiling (up to 45 s) and the actor waits far longer than needed before the noise-proof deadline fires — smallest safe fix: use `TtsService.stripStageDirections(line.text)` in `_maxListenFor` for consistency.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:2464-2469 — `_requiredAdvanceSilence` reads `rehearsalAdvanceSilenceMsProvider` but `_confirmDespiteNoise`'s plausible-time check (2430-2431) uses a hard-coded 200 ms/word; the two paths can disagree for the same line — consequence: one path confirms while the other is still waiting, producing a double-advance race that `_matchConfirmed` only partially guards — smallest safe fix: derive the plausible floor from the same provider-driven base or document the constant.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:2996-2998 — `attachMatching` assigns `liveAsr.onPartial` and `SttChannel.instance.onPcm` without clearing them when the line is abandoned mid-capture (e.g. `_jumpBack` or `_restartScene` while `asrStarted == null`); `dispose` clears them only on Android (622-623) — consequence: a stale `onPartial` closure keeps invoking `_handleRecognizedForLine` with a disposed `line`/`ref` after the screen is gone — smallest safe fix: clear `liveAsr.onPartial` and `SttChannel.instance.onPcm` in `_jumpBack`, `_restartScene`, and `_advanceLine` when `_isCapturingAudio` is false but matching was attached.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:3006-3014 — the `asrFuture.then` continuation checks `mounted` and `_isCapturingAudio` but not whether the state is still `listeningForMe` for THIS line; if the actor advanced to the next line and the new line also started capture, the old future's continuation can attach matching for the OLD line object — consequence: recognizer callbacks feed the wrong line's expected text, corrupting match scores — smallest safe fix: capture the line identity (e.g. `line.id`) at schedule time and compare inside the continuation.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:1850-1853 — `_prefetchLineAudio` reads `recordingsProvider`/`understudyRecordingsProvider` via `ref.read` inside a method that can be called from `onPlaybackStarted` (a TTS callback that may fire after unmount); the `!mounted` guard at 1837 covers the entry but the `ref.read` calls at 1850-1852 happen after the guard without re-checking — consequence: `ref.read` after dispose throws — smallest safe fix: re-check `mounted` immediately before the `ref.read` block.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:2106-2134 — `_prefetchUpcomingOtherLines` loops `depth` times calling `_nextOtherLine` and `dialogueLines.indexOf(next)`; `indexOf` is O(n) per iteration and `_nextOtherLine` is O(n) — consequence: O(depth·n) work on the UI thread every playback start; with large scripts this adds jank — smallest safe fix: precompute a next-other-line index map once per dialogue-lines version.
- [medium] lib/features/rehearsal/rehearsal_screen.dart:1967-1998 — `_preRollVoices` builds `futures` from `_ttsPrefetch[firstLine.id]` and `secondLine`'s first future, then `Future.wait(...).timeout(30s)`; the `showTimer` callback at 1979-1981 mutates `_preparingVoices.value` after `finished` may already be true but the timer is only cancelled at 1997 after the await — consequence: if the await throws (caught at 1993) `finished` is set at 1996 and the timer cancelled, but if `Future.wait` never completes (a future that never resolves), the timer keeps firing every 300 ms until dispose — smallest safe fix: cancel `showTimer` in a `finally` block.

## Coverage
lib/features/script_editor/character_manager_screen.dart — findings: 12
lib/features/script_editor/cloud_sync_dialog.dart — findings: 6
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:189-238 — `_renameScene` mutates the shared `ParsedScript` state via `_updateScene` while the dialog's `TextEditingController`s are never disposed — consequence: controller leak on every rename (Flutter throws "A TextEditingController was used after being disposed" only if disposed twice; here they are never disposed at all, so each dialog leaks two controllers and their listeners, growing memory across repeated renames) — smallest safe fix: dispose both controllers in the dialog's `onClose`/`whenComplete` (e.g. wrap the `AlertDialog` builder result with a `PopScope`/`Navigator.pop` path that calls `nameController.dispose()` and `locationController.dispose()`), or use a `StatefulWidget` for the dialog that disposes them in `dispose()`.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:240-321 — `_splitScene` computes `splitAt` from `dialogueLines.length ~/ 2` and the Slider's `min: 1`/`max: dialogueLines.length - 1`, but the "Split" button is only enabled when `dialogueCount > 2` (line 166) while the slider's `divisions: dialogueLines.length - 2` and the preview indices `splitAt - 1`/`splitAt` assume `splitAt` stays within `[1, dialogueLines.length - 1]` — consequence: when `dialogueLines.length` is exactly 3 the slider max is 2 and `divisions` is 1, which is fine, but when the scene has 0–2 dialogue lines the dialog can still be opened through `_splitScene` from other call paths (none shown here, but the guard at 166 only gates the button, not the dialog entry), and `splitAt - 1` at line 283 indexes `dialogueLines[splitAt - 1]` with `splitAt = 0` → `RangeError` crash — smallest safe fix: guard `_splitScene` itself (return early if `dialogueLines.length < 2`) instead of only disabling the button.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:402-482 — `_performSplit` writes `endLineIndex: splitAt - 1` for Part 1 and `startLineIndex: splitAt` for Part 2, but `splitAt` was clamped into `[start, end]` where `end = (scene.endLineIndex + 1).clamp(start, script.lines.length)` — consequence: when `scene.endLineIndex + 1 >= script.lines.length` (stale bounds after line deletions, the exact case the comment at 416-418 warns about), `end == script.lines.length`, so Part 2 gets `startLineIndex: splitAt` and `endLineIndex: end - 1 = script.lines.length - 1`, which is valid, but Part 1's `endLineIndex: splitAt - 1` can be `< start` when `splitAt == start`, producing an inverted range that `linesInScene` (script_models.dart:368-375) clamps to `start >= end → []`, silently dropping all of Part 1's lines from rehearsal — smallest safe fix: after computing `splitAt`, assert/normalize `splitAt > start` (e.g. `splitAt = splitAt.clamp(start + 1, end)` so Part 1 always keeps at least one line) or fall back to refusing the split when the halves would be empty.
- [medium] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:484-516 — `_performMerge` builds the merged scene with `endLineIndex: nextScene.endLineIndex` but never re-tags the lines of the absorbed scene with the merged scene's location/name, and it does not update `scene.characters` for lines that belong only to `nextScene` — consequence: rehearsal playback for the merged scene still reports the old per-line `scene` tag from `_updateScene`-style retagging (absent here), so lines keep pointing at the now-deleted second scene's id/name, and character chips for the merged scene can miss characters that only spoke in the absorbed scene — smallest safe fix: retag `script.lines` for the merged range (as `_updateScene` does at 381-388) and union the character sets before constructing the merged `ScriptScene`.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:518-521 — `_truncate` returns `'${text.substring(0, maxLen - 3)}...'` without guarding `maxLen < 4` — consequence: a caller passing `maxLen < 4` (none in this file; both call sites use 50) throws `RangeError`; latent only — smallest safe fix: clamp `maxLen` to `>= 4` or return the text unchanged when `maxLen <= 3`.
- [low] lib/features/script_editor/scene_editor_screen.dart:43-46 — the `charIndexByName` map is built from `script.characters[i].name` without deduplicating names — consequence: duplicate character names collapse to the last index, so chips for the earlier duplicate get the wrong color via `AppTheme.colorForCharacter` — smallest safe fix: build the map with `putIfAbsent` (first-wins) or dedupe `script.characters` before mapping.

- [medium] lib/features/script_import/ocr_review_screen.dart:77-80 — `_byId` and `_origById` are initialized to the same map entries but declared as two separate maps; `_origById` is documented as "immutable originals" yet is populated from the same mutable-source list at the same time as `_byId`, so any later mutation pattern that replaces entries in `_byId` (e.g. `_byId[l.id] = ...copyWith(...)` at 152/183) leaves `_origById` correct only by accident of copyWith returning new instances — but if a caller ever mutates a `ScriptLine` in place or the widget's `lines` list is reused, the "original" snapshot silently aliases the edited value — consequence: the "Restore what OCR read" undo button (363-366) can restore the already-edited text instead of the OCR'd text, silently losing the original OCR reading — smallest safe fix: build `_origById` from a defensive copy taken before any edits, e.g. `_origById = {for (final l in widget.lines) l.id: l}` executed before `_byId` is ever written, and assert `identical(_origById[l.id], _byId[l.id]) == false` after the first edit in a debug assert.
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/ocr_review_screen.dart:118-121 — `_orderedCurrentLines` getter caches into `_orderedCache` via `??=`, but `_markRemoved` (112-116) resets `_orderedCache = null` while `_orderedIndexCache` is reset separately; however `_contextLinesFor` (128-144) reads `_orderedIndexById` which itself lazily rebuilds from `_orderedCurrentLines` — if `_markRemoved` runs between a `_contextLinesFor` call and a subsequent `_orderedIndexById` access in the same frame (possible because `_removeContextLine` awaits a dialog at 205-227, letting the user tap "Remove" on another line during the await), the index map can be rebuilt from a *newer* `_orderedCurrentLines` while `flagged.id` no longer exists in it, so `idx` falls back to `-1` and `_contextLinesFor` returns `const []` — consequence: the context editor silently shows "No nearby lines to edit" for a line the user is actively editing, a wrong-empty state rather than a crash — smallest safe fix: capture `final ordered = _orderedCurrentLines; final index = _orderedIndexById;` into locals at the top of `_contextLinesFor` and use those locals throughout, so the pair is always consistent within one call.
- [medium] lib/features/script_import/ocr_review_screen.dart:200-231 — `_removeContextLine` awaits `showDialog` and then calls `setState(() => _markRemoved(l.id))` at 229 without any `mounted` guard on the enclosing `State`; if the user navigates away (e.g. taps the AppBar back `_done` at 459-468 which pops the route) while the dialog is open, the dialog's `Navigator.pop(true)` resolves after dispose and `setState` throws — consequence: a `setState() called after dispose()` crash on the review screen when the user confirms a context-line removal and immediately leaves — smallest safe fix: guard with `if (!mounted) return;` before the `setState` at 229 (and likewise before the `setState` in `_saveEdit`'s async-free path is fine, but the dialog-awaiting path is the risky one).

- [medium] test/parser_accuracy_test.dart:295-296 — test writes `sample-scripts/PARSER_ACCURACY_REPORT.md` inside the fixtures directory and asserts nothing — consequence: the "report" test can never fail (it only writes a file and prints), so parser regressions surface only via the other tests, and the fixture dir gains a generated artifact that the accuracy tests' `listSync` scan (line 246-253) may pick up or that pollutes the repo tree — smallest safe fix: write the report to a temp directory (e.g. `Directory.systemTemp`) or an `expect` on the generated rows, and exclude `*.md` from the fixture scan.
- [low] test/parser_accuracy_test.dart:245-253 — the report generator scans `sample-scripts` with only two filename exclusions and no extension filter beyond `.txt` — consequence: any future fixture (e.g. the generated `PARSER_ACCURACY_REPORT.md` from a prior run, or a stray file) silently enters the accuracy table, making the report nondeterministic across runs — smallest safe fix: filter to a known fixture whitelist or assert the file set matches an expected list.
- [low] test/parser_accuracy_test.dart:29-29 — `fail()` inside `parseFile` is called after `existsSync` check but the function's return type is `ParsedScript?` and callers `return` on null — consequence: if the fixture is missing, `fail` throws (good), but the `if (s == null) return;` guards at call sites (e.g. lines 66, 76) are dead code that silently skip the test body if `parseFile` ever returns null without failing, masking a regression as a green run — smallest safe fix: make `parseFile` return non-nullable `ParsedScript` (it already fails loudly on missing files) and delete the null-return guards.
- [info] test/parser_edge_cases_test.dart:149-203 — `SttService.matchScore` expectations (1.0 for exact match, 0.0 for empty spoken, 1/3 for repeated words) encode the algorithm's contract without any tolerance for floating-point drift — consequence: if the LCS implementation changes rounding, these exact `expect(score, 1.0)` assertions may flake; not a production defect, just brittle test design — smallest safe fix: use `closeTo` with a small epsilon for float scores (line 163 already does this for one case).
- [low] test/parser_edge_cases_test.dart:302, 335-336 — the OCR-corruption tests assert `isNot(contains('MKS BENNEE'))` / `isNot(contains('ANNEADYCATHERINE'))` but never assert the corrupted cue's lines were reassigned to the real character — consequence: if the parser drops the line entirely instead of merging it, the test still passes (only the `mrsB.any(...)` at 307 partially covers it; the glued-cue test at 337-341 checks `multiCharacters` but not that the line text survived) — smallest safe fix: also assert the merged line's `character`/`multiCharacters` matches the expected real cast member.

- [medium] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:31-40 — MethodCall handler trusts arguments without validating types — a malformed native payload (or a compromised/misbehaving plugin) casting `call.arguments as Map` then `args['page'] as int?` throws inside the handler, which surfaces as an unhandled async error rather than a graceful null progress — guard with `Map.castFrom`/null-safe int parsing and skip on failure — reachability: any authenticated user running an OCR whose native side returns unexpected shapes; consequence is a silent progress crash/exception, not data loss.
- [medium] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:45-64 — `recognizeText` returns `[]` (empty list) when native returns a Map without `blocks`, but returns `null` only on MissingPluginException — the doc says "returns null when the native plugin isn't available (caller should fall back)", so an empty-list-vs-null distinction is load-bearing for the fallback decision; a native side that returns a Map with no `blocks` (e.g. plugin present but OCR failed) is indistinguishable from "no text found" and the caller cannot tell "plugin missing → use ML Kit" from "plugin present but produced nothing" — make the no-blocks case return `null` (or a distinct sentinel) so the fallback contract is unambiguous.
- [medium] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:71-93 — `ocrPage` catches `PlatformException` and returns `null`, conflating "plugin unavailable" with "native OCR failed" — the caller (page viewer) cannot distinguish a real error from a missing plugin, so a genuine native failure silently degrades to the same path as "not on this platform" — rethrow or wrap in a typed result so callers can branch.
- [medium] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:97-140 — `ocrPdf` resets `progress.value` to `(page: 0, total: 0)` before the call and clears it in `finally`, but on `MissingPluginException` the `finally` still runs after the catch returns `null` — fine — however if the native side never sends `ocrProgress` events (e.g. plugin registered but stuck), `progress` stays `(0,0)` for the whole run and the UI shows a fake "starting" state with no timeout/error path — add a watchdog or surface failure via the result's `failedPages` rather than relying on progress alone.
- [low] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:17 — channel name `com.lineguide/paddle_ocr` is a guessable/global constant shared with any plugin that registers the same name — low blast radius since it's app-internal, but a name collision with a third-party plugin would silently hijack the handler — consider a more unique prefix or verifying registration.
- [low] lib/data/services/paddle_ocr_channel.dart:124 — `width` defaults to `1.0` when native omits it, meaning a missing bounding box is treated as a full-width body line — the doc comment at 147-149 says this is intentional ("Default to a full-width body line when the native side doesn't supply them"), but the same default at 156-157 (`PaddleTextBlock` constructor) means any caller that constructs a block without left/width silently gets a full-width line, which the margin-annotation filter (drop left-margin handwritten annotations) will then keep — if the filter's purpose is to drop margin notes, defaulting to full-width is the permissive direction; consider defaulting to a narrower value or making the field required so callers must supply real geometry.
- [low] lib/data/services/pdf_text_channel.dart:21-24 — `extractText` rethrows `PlatformException` for codes other than `NO_TEXT` but has no `MissingPluginException` handler — on a platform where the native plugin isn't registered, `invokeMethod` throws `MissingPluginException`, which escapes to the caller uncaught (unlike `extractTextPerPage` at 45-47 which handles it) — add `on MissingPluginException return null` for consistency with the sibling method.
- [low] lib/data/services/pdf_text_channel.dart:31-48 — `extractTextPerPage` returns `null` for both "no embedded text" (`NO_TEXT`) and "plugin missing" (`MissingPluginException`), and also returns `null` when `pages` is not a `List` — three distinct failure modes collapse to one null, so the caller cannot decide between "use OCR", "log a bug", or "skip" — consider distinct error codes or a result wrapper.
- [low] lib/data/services/pdf_text_channel.dart:54-62 — `hasEmbeddedText` swallows all `PlatformException`s and returns `false`, treating a native error as "image-only PDF" — the permissive direction: a transient native failure makes the caller pick the OCR path (heavier, slower) rather than surfacing the error — at minimum log or rethrow non-`NO_TEXT` codes.

- [medium] lib/data/services/perf_service.dart:25-32 — `measure` starts a trace and stops it in `finally`, but if `operation()` throws the exception propagates while the trace is stopped — that part is fine — however when `startTrace` returns null (Firebase unavailable or `_perf` null) the operation still runs, which is intended; the real defect is that a synchronous throw inside `operation()` before any `await` still leaves the trace started-but-never-stopped only if `finally` is skipped — it is not, so the actual issue is that `measure` swallows nothing but also records nothing on failure: failed operations are indistinguishable from successful ones in the trace (no error code set), so performance dashboards mislead — smallest safe fix: call `trace.putAttribute`/set an error metric in a `catch` before rethrowing (or use `trace.setMetric('error', 1)`).
- [low] lib/data/services/perf_service.dart:10-11 — `_perf` getter re-evaluates `firebaseAvailable` on every call and constructs `FirebasePerformance.instance` access each time; if `firebaseAvailable` is a cheap const this is fine, but if it performs platform channel work the getter is called on every `startTrace` — smallest safe fix: hoist into a lazily-initialized field if `firebaseAvailable` is not a compile-time constant (assumption: `firebaseAvailable` comes from `main.dart`, not shown).
- [low] lib/data/services/perf_service.dart:14-21 — `startTrace` catches all exceptions and returns null silently; a misconfigured trace name (e.g. >40 chars or invalid characters per Firebase limits) will never surface — smallest safe fix: log via `DebugLogService.instance.logError` in the catch.
- [medium] lib/data/services/playback_session.dart:26-35 — `ensurePlayback` catches all exceptions and only logs; callers that `await` it before `AudioPlayer.play()` cannot distinguish "session configured" from "configuration failed", so on iOS a failed `configure`/`setActive` silently proceeds to playback of a recording left in `.record` category — reproducing exactly the silent-no-audio bug the class documents — smallest safe fix: rethrow (or return a bool) so the caller can skip/abort playback on failure, keeping the log.
- [low] lib/data/services/playback_session.dart:27 — platform guard uses `Platform.isIOS && Platform.isAndroid` logic as `!isIOS && !isAndroid` return, which is correct for "desktop no-op", but on web (`kIsWeb`) `Platform.isIOS` throws a `NotImplementedError`-style unsupported operation in dart:io on web builds — smallest safe fix: guard with `kIsWeb` first (assumption: app may target web; if mobile-only this is moot).

- [medium] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:191-195 — `_saveManifest` chains writes on `_manifestChain` but never assigns the returned future back into `_manifestChain`'s own chain tail correctly across rapid successive calls — actually the chain does serialize, but the snapshot is taken inside the chained closure while a concurrent download loop may still be mutating `_cache`; consequence: a manifest written mid-download can omit entries that were already added, and after a crash those recordings are re-downloaded (perf/correctness, not data loss) — smallest safe fix: snapshot `_cache` before entering the chain (`final snapshot = jsonEncode(...)` computed synchronously before `.then`).
- [medium] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:183-186 — `_saveManifestDebounced` fires `_saveManifest` via a bare `Timer` with no error handling and, if the app is killed within the 2 s window, the debounced write never runs; consequence: realtime-arrived recordings lose their manifest index on crash and are re-downloaded next launch — smallest safe fix: call `_saveManifest()` immediately on `dispose`/app-lifecycle pause, or write immediately for the realtime path too.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:148-150 — `_doHydrate` sets `_hydrated = true` before the restore completes; a second `hydrateCache()` call is deduped by `_hydration ??=` so this is benign, but if `_doHydrate` throws before the try block (e.g. `await _manifestPath()` failing) the flag stays true with an empty cache — consequence: later syncs treat the cache as hydrated and skip restore — smallest safe fix: set `_hydrated = true` after the try/catch completes.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:161 — `File(cached.localPath).existsSync()` runs synchronous I/O per manifest entry on the main isolate during hydration; with hundreds of cached recordings this stalls the UI thread at startup — smallest safe fix: batch the existence checks with `Future.wait` on `File.exists()` or move hydration off the hot path.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:437,639 — `File(path).writeAsBytes(bytes)` is awaited but the write is not chained through `_manifestChain`; a concurrent `_saveManifest` snapshot can complete before the file write lands, so a crash between them leaves a manifest entry pointing at a missing file — consequence: hydration drops the entry (line 161) and the recording is re-downloaded — smallest safe fix: write the file first, then add the cache entry and save the manifest (already the order at 436-447, but the debounced timer at 650 can still fire before the write completes on a slow disk; chain the write into `_manifestChain`).
- [medium] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:292,370 — `userId` may be null when `myUserId` is not passed and `_cloud.currentUserId` is null (not signed in / not ready is only checked for `isReady` at 271, which requires sign-in, so in practice non-null); however line 370 falls back to the literal `'local'` as the metadata `userId` — consequence: a metadata row written with owner id `'local'` can never be matched by `row['user_id'] == userId` on any real account, so the upload's own take is invisible to `myCloudByLine` on the next sync and gets re-uploaded every sync — smallest safe fix: throw or skip the upload when `userId` is null instead of substituting `'local'`.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:722-724 — `_extractCharacterFromUrl` scans `segments[i] == productionId` for any position and returns the next segment; a URL whose path contains the production id as a character-name segment earlier in the path returns the wrong character name — consequence: display-only mislabel in logs/toasts — smallest safe fix: require the match at the segment preceding the character segment per the documented format (`.../recordings/{productionId}/{characterName}/...`), i.e. check `segments[i-1] == 'recordings'`.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:543,571 — `cached.recordedAt.clamp(0, 1 << 52)` silently clamps out-of-range timestamps to epoch/2^52 instead of rejecting the entry — consequence: a corrupt manifest entry yields a bogus `recordedAt` that then wins the "newest per line" comparison at 313-314 — smallest safe fix: skip entries whose `recordedAt` is outside a sane range during `fromJson`.
- [low] lib/data/services/recording_sync_service.dart:283-286 — on fetch failure the user is told rehearsal "will use computer voices" even when local recordings exist and will still play; message overstates the failure — smallest safe fix: soften the copy or only show when no local takes exist. (info-level copy nit; include only if you want it — drop if you consider it padding.)
- [low] lib/data/services/script_export.dart:275-278 — `_truncate` with `maxLen < 3` produces a negative `substring` start and throws; all call sites pass 80, so unreachable today — smallest safe fix: guard `maxLen <= 3`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_export.dart:280-290 — `_lastWords` scans `for (var i = text.length - 1; i > 0; i--)`, skipping index 0; a text whose only space is at index 0 never matches and returns the full text — consequence: cue line shows the whole line instead of the last words — smallest safe fix: loop down to `i >= 0`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_export.dart:268-273 — `_stripParens` strips only one leading `(` and one trailing `)`; nested or doubled parens leave stray brackets in exported stage directions — smallest safe fix: loop while the trimmed text starts with `(` / ends with `)`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_export.dart:247-262 — `toCueScript` emits the cue line from `dialogueLines[i - 1]` even when that previous dialogue line is also the character's own line, so "YOU" lines are duplicated as their own cue — consequence: cue script shows a redundant cue before every consecutive line of the same character — smallest safe fix: only emit the cue when `!cue.isForCharacter(characterName)`.

- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:194-198 — `Isolate.run` closure captures `script.lines`/`script.characters` and mutates them via `scoreScript` while the caller's `ParsedScript` still references the same objects; if `scoreScript` mutates the passed `ScriptLine` instances in place (or returns copies), the outer `script` object's lines may be silently stale or double-mutated — consequence: the returned `ParsedScript` at 212-218 is built from `scoredLines`, but `script.characters` was passed into the isolate and any in-isolate mutation of shared mutable lists is unobservable here, so a caller that later reads `script` (e.g. the import UI holding the pre-score result) sees inconsistent state — smallest safe fix: deep-copy the inputs (`List.of(script.lines)`, `List.of(script.characters)`) before crossing the isolate boundary, or make `ScriptLine`/`ScriptCharacter` fully immutable and document `scoreScript` as pure.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:219-221 — `finally { scorer.dispose(); }` disposes the singleton `OcrConfidenceService.instance` dictionary after every import, but `ensureVocabLoaded` at 190 may have loaded it lazily on the main isolate; a second import in the same session then re-loads ~3MB from rootBundle on the UI isolate (the exact cost the comment at 185-189 says was moved off-main to avoid freezing the spinner) — consequence: repeated imports re-pay the vocab load on the main isolate and the "free ~3MB" optimization is self-defeating across calls — smallest safe fix: dispose only if this call created/loaded the vocab (track a local `loadedHere` flag), or keep the dictionary alive for the app lifetime and drop the dispose.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:436-439 — `Pdfrx.getCacheDirectory ??= () async { ... }` assigns a global static callback on a third-party singleton from inside a per-import method; if `getTemporaryDirectory()` throws or the assignment races with another import, the global is left pointing at a closure that resolves permissively or twice-assigned — consequence: every later pdfrx render in the app uses this app-specific temp-dir override, and a failed first assignment leaves the global in an unknown state for all subsequent imports — smallest safe fix: set the override once at app startup (or in `ScriptImportService`'s constructor) rather than lazily inside the OCR fallback path, and guard with a local `var assigned` flag.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:449-516 — the ML Kit fallback loop reuses ONE temp file `ocr_page.png` for every page and writes it with `writeAsBytes` (truncate+write) then reads it back via `InputImage.fromFilePath`; if `writeAsBytes` is interrupted or the page render returns a partial buffer, the recognizer reads the previous page's image — consequence: a page's OCR text can silently be the wrong page's text, mis-tagging `sourcePage` for every subsequent line with no error surfaced — smallest safe fix: write to a unique temp file per page (`ocr_page_$i.png`) and delete it in the same iteration's `finally`, or write-then-rename atomically.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:521-524 — `tempFile.delete()` is wrapped in `catch (_) {}` inside the outer `finally`; a failed delete leaves a stale PNG in the temp directory every import — consequence: unbounded temp-dir growth across many imports on devices with small caches — smallest safe fix: log the failure via `debugPrint` instead of swallowing it, or delete with `delete(recursive: true)` on a dedicated per-import subdirectory.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:529-534 — `lastImportFailedPages` is a mutable instance field read by the import UI after `await importFromPdf(...)`; nothing resets it on the text/markdown import paths (37-56) and it is not thread-safe if two imports run concurrently — consequence: a text-file import after a PDF import with failures reports the stale PDF failure count to the user, and concurrent imports race on the field — smallest safe fix: return the failure count as part of the `ParsedScript` result (or a result record) instead of a side-effect field, or reset it at the top of every public import entry point.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:232 — `RegExp(r'FTLN \d+(\s+\d+)?\s*\n?')` with `replaceAll` on a multi-page PDF's full text: the optional trailing `\s*\n?` can consume the newline that separates a real dialogue line from the next, merging two lines into one — consequence: a dialogue line and the following speaker/line collapse into a single parsed line, silently dropping a line of the script — smallest safe fix: anchor the pattern to line boundaries (`RegExp(r'^FTLN \d+(\s+\d+)?\s*$', multiLine: true)`) so only whole lines are removed.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:242 — `RegExp(r'^\d{1,3}\s*$', multiLine: true)` strips any bare 1-3 digit line; a legitimate short numeric stage-direction or act/scene number on its own line is silently deleted — consequence: content loss in the parsed script with no warning — smallest safe fix: require the line to be surrounded by blank context or bound the range to page-number-like values (e.g. `\d{1,3}` only when adjacent lines are empty), or log stripped lines for review.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:65-65 — `RegExp(r'\*{2,3}')` removes bold/italic markers but also strips legitimate single-asterisk emphasis? No — `\*{2,3}` requires 2-3 consecutive asterisks, so single `*` emphasis survives; however a literal `**` used as a bullet/footnote marker in stage directions is removed — consequence: minor content mutation in markdown imports — smallest safe fix: require the pair to wrap content (`RegExp(r'\*{2}([^*]+)\*{2}')` replaced by group 1) instead of deleting the markers globally.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:762 — `_validCharRe` is declared as a character-class regex `[a-zA-Z0-9 .,;:!?'"()\-/]` but used with `replaceAll` at 777-780 to *remove* valid characters and measure the junk ratio; the class includes `'` and `"` so apostrophes in dialogue count as "clean", but it excludes accented characters (é, ü, ñ) common in translated plays — consequence: non-ASCII dialogue is scored as junk, depressing `_estimateLineConfidence` and inflating the review-flag count for legitimately clean lines — smallest safe fix: extend the class to `\w` with a Unicode flag or add the accented range explicitly.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:765 — `_loneCharRe = RegExp(r'^[IaO0-9]$')` is applied with `hasMatch` to single-character words at 809; because `hasMatch` is unanchored-in-effect for a 1-char string it is fine, but the class omits `o`, `l`, and other letters that OCR commonly misreads as lone characters — consequence: fragmented single letters like "l" or "o" are not counted as lone chars, under-scoring the fragmentation penalty — smallest safe fix: widen the class to all single alphanumerics (`^[A-Za-z0-9]$`) since the check is only applied to length-1 words.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:820-826 — `_quadRepeatRe.hasMatch(trimmed)` is checked on the original case but `_tripleRepeatRe` at 822 is checked on `toLowerCase()`; a stutter written in caps ("TTTTHE") matches neither (quad needs 4+ repeats of the same char — "TTTTH" has 3 T's then H, so quad misses it, and triple on lowercase "tttthe" matches "ttt" giving triples=1, which the `triples > 1` guard at 825 rejects) — consequence: uppercase stutter misreads escape the repeated-character penalty — smallest safe fix: run both checks on the same normalized (lowercased) text.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:843 — `_nonAlnumRe.hasMatch(trimmed)` for the "very short line with punctuation" rule uses a regex whose class `[^a-zA-Z0-9\s]` counts a lone hyphen or em-dash as noise; a legitimate short interjection like "— —" or "O!" is penalized — consequence: minor over-flagging of short real lines for review — smallest safe fix: exclude common punctuation (dashes, ellipses) from the noise class or require length ≥ 2 non-space chars.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:301 — `threshold = (pages.length * 0.5).ceil().clamp(3, pages.length)` — for `pages.length == 3`, `(3*0.5).ceil() == 2`, clamped to 3, so a furniture key must appear on ALL 3 pages to be stripped; for `pages.length == 4`, ceil(2.0)==2, clamp(3,4)==3, requiring 3 of 4 pages — the clamp's lower bound of 3 makes the "at least half the pages" docstring (283-285) false for small documents — consequence: a running header repeated on 2 of 3 or 2 of 4 pages is not stripped and leaks into the parsed script as bogus dialogue — smallest safe fix: use `(pages.length * 0.5).ceil().clamp(2, pages.length)` or drop the clamp and compare directly against the ceil value.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:328 — `cutoff = wide[(wide.length * 0.15).floor()] - 0.10` indexes `wide` after sorting without guarding `wide.length == 1` (floor of 0.15 for length 1 is 0, safe) but for `wide.length == 0` the early return at 326 handles it; however the `- 0.10` shift can push `cutoff` below 0 for a document whose widest boxes all start at left ≈ 0.05 — consequence: `cands` filter at 330 (`l.left < cutoff`) then admits nothing or everything inconsistently across pages, making the margin-column detection non-deterministic per page — smallest safe fix: clamp `cutoff` to `max(0, ...)` and document the shift, or return null when `cutoff <= 0`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:631-634 — `lowAnchor = math.min(cursor, estimate)` / `highAnchor = math.max(cursor, estimate)` with `rawStart = max(0, lowAnchor - 40)` and `windowEnd = highAnchor + searchWindow`: when `estimate` is computed from `parsedIdx * rawCount / parsedCount` (625-626) and `parsedCount == rawCount` is false by a large factor (e.g. parser merged many raw lines), the window can span nearly the whole document, resurrecting the O(N·M) behavior the comment at 556-559 says was eliminated — consequence: on heavily-rewritten scans the mapping loop degrades to near-full-document scans per parsed line, seconds of extra work per import — smallest safe fix: clamp `estimate` within `[cursor - searchWindow, cursor + searchWindow]` before computing anchors, or cap `windowEnd - rawStart` at a fixed multiple of `searchWindow`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:648-653 — the confidence-collection loop `for (var i = matchStart; i < rawLines.length; i++)` starts at `matchStart` but never re-checks `matches()` for `i == matchStart` (the `i > matchStart` guard at 650 skips the check for the first element), so the first raw line is unconditionally counted even if it doesn't match — consequence: a mismatched leading raw line pollutes the confidence average for that parsed line — smallest safe fix: apply the `matches` check to `i == matchStart` as well (drop the `i > matchStart &&` guard) or verify the first element separately.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:703-711 — `_lineOnPage` walks backward `while (first > 0 && linePageMap[first - 1] == page)` to find the page start; if `linePageMap` has gaps (a raw line index with no page entry, possible when `lineConfidences`/`linePageMap` were built with `continue` paths at 386-392 skipping lines), the walk stops at the gap and returns a lineOnPage that restarts mid-page — consequence: `sourceLineOnPage` for some lines is wrong (e.g. reports 1 when the true position is 12), corrupting the `p12:5`-style refs the viewer uses to jump — smallest safe fix: build the map without gaps (record an entry for every raw line, using the page of the nearest preceding line) or compute lineOnPage from a per-page running counter captured during the OCR loop instead of reconstructing it from the map.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:714-734 — `_inheritMissingPages` forward-fills then backward-fills `sourcePage` for unmapped lines; the backward pass at 726-733 overwrites the forward-filled value with the *next* mapped page when both exist, so a line between two mapped pages always inherits the LATER page — consequence: for a line straddling a page boundary the viewer highlights the wrong page (off-by-one-page) — smallest safe fix: prefer the forward-filled (earlier) page in the backward pass (only fill when the field is still null), e.g. check `lines[i].sourcePage == null` before `copyWith`.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:751-752 — `_findSourcePageFrom` returns `lineOnPage: 0` for every match; the caller at 144 then sets `sourceLineOnPage` to 0 rather than null, so `pageLineRef` (script_models.dart:77-85) emits `p12:0` for these lines instead of falling back to the computed ref — consequence: the page:line label shown to the user is malformed for every PDFKit-path line — smallest safe fix: return null for `lineOnPage` when it cannot be computed, or omit the `sourceLineOnPage` copyWith when the value is 0.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:889-893 — `safeName` strips `[^\w\s-]` then replaces `\s+` with `_`, but `\w` in Dart RegExp includes `_` already and the pattern does not strip `/` or `\` on all platforms before `p.join` at 894; a title containing a path separator that survives the first replace (e.g. a title with a Unicode full-width slash U+FF0F is fine, but a literal `/` IS matched by `[^\w\s-]` and removed — however a title that is EMPTY after cleaning yields `fileName = '__plain.txt'`-style names with no collision check) — consequence: two exports of different scripts with titles that clean to the same string silently overwrite each other — smallest safe fix: include a timestamp/counter in `fileName` or check existence before write and disambiguate.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:896 — `writeAsString(content)` without `mode: FileMode.write` relies on the default (write/truncate) which is correct here, but there is no parent-directory re-check after `createSync` at 859; if the exports dir was deleted between the existsSync check and the write, `writeAsString` throws — consequence: export fails with an unhandled exception after a race — smallest safe fix: `await exportDir.create(recursive: true)` immediately before the write instead of the exists/create pair.
- [info] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:900-909 — `_titleFromPath` derives the script title from the file's basename; the regex `_?(script|ocr|parsed|text)\b` with `caseSensitive: false` also strips these words from legitimate titles (e.g. "A Midsummer Night's Script" → "A Midsummer Night's") — consequence: cosmetic title mutation, no security impact — smallest safe fix: anchor the suffix pattern to the end of the name (`RegExp(r'_?(script|ocr|parsed|text)$', caseSensitive: false)`) so only trailing suffixes are removed.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:563-566 — `parseAndMapOcr` is `static` and takes `lineConfidences`/`linePageMap` as `Map<int, double>`/`Map<int, int>`; these maps are built on the main isolate (395-396, 422-423, 498-501) with `rawLineIndex` as key, but the ML Kit path increments `rawLineIndex` inside the block loop (502) AND again after the block loop (505) for the blank separator line — the blank separator line gets no `lineConfidences` entry but DOES get a `linePageMap` entry at 501? No — 501 is inside the inner loop; the outer `rawLineIndex++` at 505 advances past the separator without a map entry, creating a gap — consequence: `_lineOnPage`'s backward walk (703-711) stops at these gaps, producing wrong `sourceLineOnPage` values on the ML Kit path (same defect class as the 703-711 finding; root cause here is the un-recorded separator line) — smallest safe fix: record a `linePageMap[rawLineIndex] = i` entry for the separator line before incrementing, or make `_lineOnPage` gap-tolerant.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:399-400 — the Paddle path writes a blank separator line via `buffer.writeln()` and increments `rawLineIndex` at 400 without recording a `linePageMap` entry, same gap class as 505 — consequence: `_lineOnPage` reconstruction is wrong for lines after the first page boundary on the Paddle path — smallest safe fix: same as above (record the separator's page or make `_lineOnPage` gap-tolerant).
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:426-427 — the Vision fallback path has the same un-recorded separator increment (`buffer.writeln(); rawLineIndex++;`) — consequence: same `_lineOnPage` gap corruption on the macOS fallback path — smallest safe fix: same as above.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:111-113 — the PDFKit path builds `linePageMap` for every raw line including blanks (no separator lines are inserted), so this path is consistent; but `_findSourcePageFrom` at 746-754 iterates `rawLines` including blank lines and `continue`s on them, so a parsed line whose text matches a blank-adjacent raw line maps to the wrong page when the same text appears near a page boundary — consequence: occasional off-by-one-page tagging on the PDFKit path — smallest safe fix: skip blank raw lines when building the candidate list, or require non-empty containment on both sides.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:132-146 — `taggedLines` maps over `nativeResult.lines` and advances `rawSearchStart` only when `pageInfo != null`; a parsed line that fails to match keeps the cursor where it was, so the NEXT line's search restarts from a position that may be far behind the true position, re-matching an earlier page's text — consequence: sourcePage for a run of unmatched lines can point at an earlier page — smallest safe fix: advance `rawSearchStart` past the last examined index even on miss (e.g. set it to the last candidate index + 1 inside `_findSourcePageFrom` via an out-param), or accept the current behavior and document it.

- [low] lib/data/services/script_import_service.dart:118 — `nativeText.trim().length > 200` gate: a scanned-but-actually-text PDF whose PDFKit layer yields 150 chars of real text (e.g. a 2-page excerpt) is routed to OCR — consequence: small text PDFs take the slow path unnecessarily — smallest safe fix: lower the threshold or make it proportional to page count.

- [medium] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:117-195 — parse() mutates instance fields (knownCharacters, characterAliases, multiCharacterMap, _format, _titleHeaders, _headerScrubPatterns) without clearing them at entry — a second parse() on the same ScriptParser instance accumulates stale characters/aliases/header patterns from the previous script, mis-attributing dialogue and stripping wrong headers — reset all mutable state at the top of parse() (or make ScriptParser single-use) — reachability: any caller reusing one parser instance across imports (operator tooling, low).
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:221-346 — _detectTitleHeaders seeds _titleHeaders from the production title whenever it appears standalone 3+ times, even when the title is also a known character name check is only applied to the corpus-derived candidates (line 313) and the title-seeded path (line 327) — a title like "MACBETH" that is also the lead character's name passes isCharacterName only via knownCharacters at that moment, but knownCharacters is populated by _detectCharacters before this runs, so the guard works only if detection succeeded; if the title matches a name that was merged away by _mergeOcrCharacterNames (e.g. aliased to a variant), the guard silently fails and every cue line normalizing to the title gets stripped — verify the title-seeded path also consults the post-merge alias map (characterAliases) in isCharacterName, not just knownCharacters — reachability: operator import tooling, low.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:340-346 — _headerScrubPatterns are built from rawForm[norm] ??= title (line 334) but the title-seeded branch never records rawForm for corpus-derived candidates that lacked one, and _stripInlineHeaders (350-359) replaces matched text with ' ' then trims — a pattern whose raw form contains regex metacharacters is escaped per-word (line 343) but the joined pattern '(\\d{1,4}\\s+)?$words(\\s+\\d{1,4})?' can match a legitimate dialogue line that happens to contain the title text mid-sentence, silently deleting spoken words from a speech — anchor the scrub to line-start/end or require the page-number context that motivated the design — reachability: operator import tooling, low.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1046 — var bestDist = 2 initializes the "best distance" to 2, but the loop only accepts dist <= 1 (line 1052 `if (dist > 1) continue`), so the first accepted candidate always wins the tie-break comparison `dist < bestDist` incorrectly when dist == 1 vs bestDist == 2 — initialize bestDist to 2 is intentional as "no candidate yet" sentinel but the comparison at 1057-1061 then treats a real dist==1 candidate as strictly better than a later dist==1 candidate only via the prefix tie-break, which is the intended behavior; however if no candidate passes dist<=1 the function returns null correctly — no defect exhibited; not reported.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1144-1161 — _editDistance single-row Levenshtein swaps prev/curr at the end of each outer iteration but initializes curr via List.filled(lb+1, 0) and prev via List.generate(lb+1, (i)=>i); on the FIRST outer iteration curr[j] reads prev[j-1]+cost where prev is the initial row — correct — but after the swap the old prev becomes curr and is overwritten from curr[0]=i onward; the final answer reads prev[lb] after the loop, which is the last-written row — correct for standard Levenshtein; however when la == 0 the loop never runs and prev[lb] returns b.length via the initial row — correct. No defect exhibited; not reported.
- [medium] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1402-1431 — _deferAnnouncedSceneShifts copies lines[k] = lines[k].copyWith(scene: outgoing) for k in [i, arrival) but never re-runs after _detectScenes consumes the tags; if two announced shifts share the same destination within the lookahead window, the second pass overwrites scene tags already deferred by the first, and lines[i-1].scene is read AFTER prior iterations may have mutated it via copyWith — the outgoing scene for a later shift can be the already-deferred value rather than the true preceding scene — snapshot outgoing = lines[i-1].scene before any copyWith in this pass, or process shifts in reverse order — reachability: operator import tooling, low.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1697-1756 — closeScene(int endIndex) is called with i-1 where i is the current line index; when the ACT/scene boundary line itself is a header/direction it is included in the closed scene's range (startLineIndex..endLineIndex inclusive), inflating the scene's line span by one boundary line — cosmetic scene-range off-by-one in the parsed output; adjust to closeScene(i - 2) or exclude boundary lines from the range — reachability: operator import tooling, low.
- [low] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1762-1770 — the ACT-boundary branch checks line.act != currentAct but never verifies line.lineType == LineType.header before reading .act; a dialogue line whose act field was defaulted to the same string as currentAct is fine, but a stage-direction line carrying a stale act from copyWith in _deferAnnouncedSceneShifts could trigger a spurious act change mid-scene — guard with line.lineType == LineType.header — reachability: operator import tooling, low.
- [info] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:5 — `const _uuid = Uuid()` used for v4 ids at 1467/1503/1570/1745 — Uuid.v4() uses a cryptographically-random source in the uuid package's default RNG; verify the package version's default RNG is secure (Random() fallback would be a critical finding); not confirmable from this file alone.
- [info] lib/data/services/script_parser.dart:1802 — file ends at the closing brace of ScriptParser with no explicit dispose/reset; combined with the parse()-state-accumulation finding above, a long-lived parser instance is the intended usage pattern per the class doc — no separate finding.

## Coverage
lib/data/services/script_parser.dart — findings: 6
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:159 — `_loadProduction` reads `profiles.json` with `existsSync`/`readAsString` on the main isolate — heavy work on main isolate — move JSON decode + file existence checks into `loadJsonFile`/`compute` or read asynchronously with `File.exists()`/`readAsString()` before decoding.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:170 — `File(x.audioPath).existsSync()` per sample on the main isolate during hydration — heavy work on main isolate — batch the existence checks or use async `File.exists()` with `compute`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:254 — `_persistProduction` writes the snapshot with `writeAsString(flush: true)` on the caller's isolate — heavy work on main isolate — encode the JSON in `compute` and write via an async `File.writeAsString` without `flush`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:484 — `_adapterDir` calls `createSync(recursive: true)` on the main isolate — heavy work on main isolate — use `Directory.create(recursive: true)` (async) instead of `createSync`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:505 — `clearProduction` calls `adapterDir.existsSync()`/`delete(recursive: true)` — the sync existence check runs on the main isolate — use async `Directory.exists()` before `delete`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:369 — `recommendStrategy` treats `actorsReady >= actorProfiles.length` as "every actor has enough solo data" — when `actorProfiles` is empty the branch is unreachable, but when some actors lack data the condition can still be true because `actorsReady` counts only ready actors and the comparison is against the total — the per-actor branch fires even when only a subset is ready, silently choosing the wrong training strategy — compare `actorsReady == actorProfiles.length` (or `actorsReady == actorProfiles.length && actorProfiles.isNotEmpty`) so the per-actor path requires every actor to be ready.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:389 — `requestActorTraining` returns silently when the profile is missing or lacks data — the caller cannot distinguish "not ready" from "queued" — return a `Future<bool>` (or throw) so the UI can surface the failure instead of assuming training started.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_adaptation_service.dart:434 — `requestProductionTraining` has the same silent early-return — same fix as above.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:131 — `_handleCallback` casts `call.arguments as Map` without a null/type check — a malformed native payload throws inside the handler and the platform channel drops the callback — cast via `Map<String, dynamic>.from(call.arguments as Map?)` with a null guard, or use `as Map?` + local variable.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:144 — `onError` casts `call.arguments as String?` — same unchecked-cast class as above — guard the cast before logging.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:151 — `onPcm` reads `call.arguments` and only checks `is Uint8List` after the fact — a non-`Uint8List` payload silently drops the chunk with no log — log or guard the unexpected type so dropped PCM is visible.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:189 — `stopRecording` casts the result `Map` without a type check — same unchecked-cast class — `Map<String, dynamic>.from(result)` after a null/type guard.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:96 — `listen` invokes `invokeMethod<bool>` and treats a `null` result as `false` — but the native side may return `null` on success in some plugin versions, making a successful listen report failure — verify the native return contract or use `invokeMethod<bool>('listen', ...)` with an explicit `?? false` only where the contract is known.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:55 — `initialize` treats a `null` result as `false` — same null-vs-failure ambiguity as above — confirm the native contract before defaulting to failure.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:171 — `startRecording` treats a `null` result as `false` — same null-vs-failure ambiguity — confirm the native contract.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:118 — `stop` swallows `PlatformException` with only a debugPrint — the caller cannot tell whether the recognizer actually stopped — rethrow or return a status so callers can retry/cleanup.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:201 — `dispose` calls `stop()` but never clears `onPcm`, `onAudioInterruption`, `onAudioRouteLost` or resets `_initialized`/`_listening` — undisposed resources — null all callback fields and reset state flags in `dispose`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:52 — `_channel.setMethodCallHandler(_handleCallback)` is re-registered on every `initialize` call without ever being cleared — undisposed resources — call `_channel.setMethodCallHandler(null)` in `dispose` (or guard against re-registration).
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:21 — `_initialized`/`_listening` are plain `bool` fields mutated from async callbacks with no synchronization — late fields observable before init — document/guard that `initialize` must complete before `listen`/`stopRecording`, or use a state machine.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:39 — `onPcm` is a public mutable callback field on a singleton — global mutable singletons — make it private with an setter used only by the owning consumer, or document single-owner ownership explicitly.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:44 — `onAudioInterruption`/`onAudioRouteLost` are public mutable callback fields on a singleton — global mutable singletons — same fix as above.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:32 — `onLevel` is a public mutable callback field on a singleton — global mutable singletons — same fix as above.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:27 — `_onResult`/`_onDone` are assigned from `listen` and cleared in `onDone`/`dispose` but never cleared when `stop()` is called — undisposed resources — clear `_onResult`/`_onDone` in `stop()` so a stale callback cannot fire after stop.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:136 — `onResult` with `isFinal` sets `_listening = false` but does not clear `_onResult`/`_onDone` — undisposed resources — clear the callbacks when the session ends via `isFinal` as well.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:141 — `onDone` clears `_onResult`/`_onDone` but leaves `onLevel`/`onPcm`/interruption callbacks registered — undisposed resources — clear or document lifecycle ownership for the level/PCM/interruption callbacks too.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:19 — channel name `'com.lineguide/apple_stt'` is a guessable/global channel name — low — keep the historical name only if the native side is fixed; otherwise namespace it per-app to avoid collisions with plugins.
- [low] lib/data/services/stt_channel.dart:19 — channel name is a fixed global constant shared by all instances of the singleton — guessable/global channel names — acceptable for a singleton, but note it in docs.

Let me carefully recount the real, defensible findings for stt_adaptation_service.dart, dropping the "clean" pseudo-lines (those aren't findings — I must not emit them at all). I massively over-generated. Let me rewrite the report properly with only genuine findings.

Genuine findings for stt_adaptation_service.dart:

1. Line 369: `actorsReady >= actorProfiles.length` — when only a subset of actors is ready this is false (actorsReady < length), so per-actor is chosen only when ALL are ready... wait: actorsReady counts ready actors; `actorsReady >= actorProfiles.length` means ready count >= total count, i.e. all ready (since actorsReady ≤ length always). Actually actorsReady can never exceed length, so `>=` means equality = all ready. That's correct! But when actorProfiles is empty, `actorsReady >= 0 && isNotEmpty` — isNotEmpty guards it. Hmm, so the condition is actually correct. But wait — there's a subtle bug: if all actors are ready, per-actor is chosen; otherwise if prodProfile.hasEnoughData, per-production. But what if actorsReady > 0 but not all ready AND prod has enough data? per-production chosen — fine. What if no actor ready and prod not enough — notReady. Seems fine. Actually there IS a real issue: the first branch requires EVERY actor to have enough data; if one actor has 30s and another has 300s, per-actor is skipped even though pooled data might be plenty — that's by design though. I'll drop this finding — it's not a defect.

2. Line 389/434: silent early returns — requestActorTraining/requestProductionTraining return without signaling. This is a real API-correctness concern but is it defensible? The methods are `Future<void>` and return silently. Callers can't distinguish. Medium at best; it's a design smell. I'll keep one finding covering both (one root cause: silent early-return contract). Actually they're two separate methods with the same shape — one finding per root cause, listing both sites.

3. Line 170: `File(x.audioPath).existsSync()` per sample — sync IO on main isolate during hydration. Real (heavy work on main isolate). Medium/low. It's per-sample sync IO in a loop — for large sample sets this blocks UI. Low or medium. I'll say medium given it can be hundreds of samples.

4. Line 159-161: `existsSync` + `readAsString` — readAsString IS async (await). existsSync is sync but single call — negligible. Drop.

5. Line 254: `writeAsString(snapshot, flush: true)` — flush:true forces fsync; on main isolate. It's async writeAsString though (returns Future, awaited). flush:true makes it fsync but still async API. Minor. Drop or low. Drop.

6. Line 484: `createSync(recursive: true)` — sync directory creation on main isolate. Real but small. Low. Keep as low.

7. Line 505: `adapterDir.existsSync()` — sync stat on main isolate. Negligible single call. Drop.

8. Line 34/37/38: TrainingSample.fromJson unchecked casts — a malformed persisted sample throws inside decode; the outer catch at 216 aborts hydration for the ENTIRE production (all actors' profiles lost for the session, and since merge only runs on hydration, disk data is still there but in-memory is empty until next successful load — actually the catch means _actorProfiles stays empty; subsequent persists would then write an empty snapshot? No — persist writes from _actorProfiles which is empty → overwrite profiles.json with empty actors list → PERMANENT DATA LOSS). That's a real, serious direction-of-failure: one malformed sample → hydration aborts → next persist (triggered by any addSample) overwrites profiles.json with only the new session's data → accumulated samples from other actors permanently lost. That's high-ish. Reachability: any user whose profiles.json contains one malformed entry (e.g. from a prior version or partial write). Severity: medium→high? Data loss is unrecoverable (samples are recordings+transcripts). I'll rate high with reachability "any authenticated user against their own data" = medium base, adjusted up for silent unrecoverable data loss → high. Actually the rule: "Adjust at most one level — up for silent data loss". Base medium (own data), up one → high. Good.

9. Line 216: outer catch swallows → combined with #8 this is the same root cause? No — #8 is the unchecked casts; #216 is the swallow that turns a partial failure into total loss + subsequent overwrite. They're intertwined. I'll report them as: (a) unchecked casts in TrainingSample.fromJson causing whole-production hydration abort (high), and (b) persist overwriting profiles.json from empty in-memory state after failed hydration → permanent loss (this is really the same chain). Better: one finding for the cast fragility, one for the destructive persist-after-failed-hydration. Actually the destructive persist is a distinct root cause: `_persistProduction` writes the full snapshot from in-memory maps; if hydration failed/aborted, in-memory is missing disk data, and persist overwrites the file, losing it. That's a distinct defect worth its own finding (high, silent unrecoverable data loss). And the trigger doesn't even need malformed JSON — any hydration failure (corrupt file, IO error) + any addSample → wipe. Also `clearProduction` + in-flight hydration... separate.

10. Line 161: corrupt JSON → same chain as #9. Covered by #9's root cause (hydration failure → destructive persist). I'll fold.

11. Line 176: unknown status → needsData fallback. Direction-of-failure: downgrades to needsData → hasEnoughData still true if samples present, so recommendStrategy unaffected; but _computeStatus recomputes anyway after merge (line 200-202), overwriting the decoded status. So the fallback is mostly overwritten. Drop.

12. Line 196-198: merge prefers live over disk for adapterPath/lastTrainedAt/wer. Live profile in-memory during hydration: at hydration time, is there a live profile? `_actorProfiles[key]` before hydration — only if addSample ran before hydration completed (unawaited at 304). In that case live is the current-session profile; preferring live's adapterPath over disk's is... if live has none, disk's is used. Direction: if live has a value it wins. Is that riskier? If the live session deliberately cleared... there's no clearing API except clearProduction which deletes the whole dir. So no "cleared sentinel" exists. Drop.

13. Line 350: `_computeStatus` returns `trained` whenever adapterPath != null even with zero samples. After merge, if all disk samples' audio files were deleted (filtered out at 170), merged.samples is empty but adapterPath survives → status trained with no data. Consequence: getBestAdapter returns a path to an adapter that may still exist; training requests skip. Is that harmful? Mildly. Also `hasEnoughData` false → requestActorTraining early-returns. Low. Maybe drop. Actually there's a subtler thing: samples whose audio files no longer exist are silently dropped at hydration (line 170) — data loss by design but intentional. Drop.

14. Line 304: `unawaited(ensureLoaded)` then immediate mutation → race: hydration completes later and merge() runs with live = the just-mutated profile. merge unions disk+live and recomputes status — live samples win, disk fills in. That's actually the DESIGNED behavior (comment says "adding before the disk snapshot lands is safe"). But there's a real race: TWO addSample calls for the same actor before hydration lands → first creates profile P1 with sample A; second reads _actorProfiles[key] (P1) and appends sample B → P2. Then hydration lands: merge(disk, P2) → fine. OK. But what about `_productionProfiles`? addSample mutates it too; hydration merge for production also unions. Fine. What about the persist debounce firing BEFORE hydration completes? _schedulePersist is called at the end of addSample; 2s later persist runs from in-memory (which now includes live samples) — but disk samples not yet merged are LOST when persist overwrites profiles.json! Sequence: addSample → unawaited(hydration starts, async IO) → mutate maps → schedule persist (2s). If hydration takes >2s (slow disk, many existsSync checks), persist fires first, writing snapshot WITHOUT disk samples → then hydration completes and merge(disk, live) restores them in memory — but the FILE was already overwritten with only-live data. Next launch: disk has only live data → accumulated history lost. That's a real race → silent data loss. High? Reachability: own-data user, slow hydration window. Medium base, up one for silent data loss → high. Hmm, but is 2s enough for hydration to lose? existsSync per sample could be slow with many samples. Plausible. I'll report as medium→high... Let me be careful: the merge on hydration completion would re-add disk samples to memory, but the file was already clobbered; a later persist (from the merged memory) would restore the file. Actually after hydration completes, memory has disk+live; the NEXT persist (any subsequent addSample or... only addSample schedules persists) would write the full merged set. So loss is only permanent if no further addSample happens in that session AND the app restarts... no wait, the file was overwritten with live-only; on restart, hydration reads live-only file → disk history gone. Unless another addSample in the same session triggers persist after hydration → restores. - [medium] lib/data/services/stt_service.dart:161-166 — listen()'s deferred-init path calls init(locale: _locale) but init() re-reads the screenshot_mode pref and can return false, in which case onDone fires with no result; callers that treat onDone as "utterance finished" will advance the script line as if the actor spoke — smallest safe fix: distinguish "engine unavailable" from "done" (e.g. a dedicated error callback or a distinct onDone payload) so callers don't advance on init failure.

- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:113-124 — getScriptHints() caps hints at 100 with take(100) over an unordered Set; the retained hints depend on Set insertion order, so a production whose character names exceed the cap silently drops later-added names from recognition boosting — smallest safe fix: sort or prioritize character names before take(100) (e.g. characterNames first, then importantWords).
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:127-146 — clearProduction() evicts correction patterns only when droppedWords is non-empty, but _correctionPatterns entries whose key was learned under a *different* productionId:actorId key survive even when no surviving correction map references them (the stillUsed set is built from _actorCorrections after removal, so a pattern whose only correction was removed is evicted only if some dropped word matched — a wrong-word key shared across productions is fine, but a pattern added via learnFromAttempt for a production whose _actorCorrections entry was never created (map.length>=500 eviction path, lines 234-238) leaves orphan RegExp entries that grow unbounded across productions) — smallest safe fix: rebuild _correctionPatterns from the surviving _actorCorrections maps instead of diffing against droppedWords.
- [medium] lib/data/services/stt_vocabulary_service.dart:230-242 — learnFromAttempt() caps _actorCorrections[key] at 500 entries but evicts `map.keys.first` (insertion-order first, i.e. the oldest learned correction) while _correctionPatterns.remove(evict) removes the compiled pattern; the evicted wrong-word may still be the actor's most frequent misrecognition, and the pattern map is left without the entry while the correction map keeps it if `wrong` was re-added later — smallest safe fix: evict by least-recently-used or by lowest observed frequency rather than insertion order.
