- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:114 — `supa.currentUser!.id` force-unwrap without in-scope null check — if the user signs out (or the session is cleared) between `isReady` being checked at the start of `_processQueue` and `saveMetadata` running after a long upload, this throws and the catch at 407 treats a successful upload as a failed one, incrementing retryCount and re-queuing a job whose metadata was already saved — check `supa.currentUser != null` before the call and pass a nullable/early-return instead of `!`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:470 — `2 << nextRetry.retryCount.clamp(0, 4)` computes the backoff delay from the FIRST failed job's retryCount, not the job being scheduled — all failed jobs get the same delay and the timer's delay never grows with subsequent failures, so exponential backoff is effectively linear/constant — compute the delay from the job with the highest retryCount (or per-job timers).
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:249 — `queuedKeys.add(key)` mutates the set while iterating `restored`, so a second persisted job for the same line is silently dropped even when the first restored copy was skipped for a missing local file — a duplicate queued job whose file still exists can be lost depending on list order — build the key set from live jobs only and check membership with `contains` instead of `add`.
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:388 — `file.lengthSync()` on the UI isolate inside the upload loop — a large audio file on slow storage blocks the main thread per attempt — use `await file.length()` (or `file.stat()`).
- [low] lib/data/services/sync_queue.dart:247 — `File(job.localPath).existsSync()` synchronous disk check per restored job at startup — with many queued jobs this adds main-thread IO during launch — use async `exists()` inside the serialized chain.
- [medium] lib/data/services/tts_service.dart:446 — `await _audioPlayer.stop()` runs unconditionally on the system-TTS fallback path even when Kokoro playback is mid-flight for a *newer* generation — a stale speak() that lost the gen race still stops the active player, cutting off the newer line's audio — guard the stop with the generation check (`if (gen == _speakGen)`) before falling back.

- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:36-39 — `_prefs` is a mutable field on a global singleton initialized via `??=` inside an async getter; two concurrent `_preferences` reads before the first `getInstance()` completes can each call `SharedPreferences.getInstance()` and interleave with the mutation-chain writes — consequence: a `setOverride` write can land on a different prefs instance than the later `getOverrides` read, silently dropping the just-written override (the exact lost-update the `_serialized` chain exists to prevent) — fix: cache the Future (`Future<SharedPreferences>? _prefsFuture`) and `return _prefsFuture ??= SharedPreferences.getInstance()`.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:29-33 — `_serialized` chains only the previous future per productionId but stores `run.then((_) {}, onError: (_) {})` as the new tail, so an op that throws is swallowed from the chain while the next queued op still runs against stale in-memory state — consequence: a failed `_saveOverrides` (e.g. prefs write error) leaves subsequent `setOverride`/`removeOverride` calls reading the old map and reporting success; callers get the thrown error only if they await the returned future, and the chain itself never observes it — fix: keep the raw `run` (or a `.then(_,_catchError)` that rethrows) as the chain tail and let the returned future surface the error.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:79-82 — `getOverrides` catch-all returns `{}` on JSON parse failure — direction of failure: corrupt/stale stored JSON resolves to the permissive "no overrides" state, so every character silently falls back to the production preset and a subsequent `setOverride` write (via `_saveOverrides`) overwrites the unparsable blob with a single-entry map, permanently destroying all other characters' stored overrides — fix: on parse failure return the raw string path (or throw) so callers can surface the corruption instead of silently re-keying over it.
- [medium] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:236-241 — `getGenders` maps any unrecognized stored value to `CharacterGender.female` via the `_ =>` default — direction of failure: a typo'd or corrupted gender entry resolves to the permissive default (female voice pool) rather than surfacing the bad state — fix: skip unknown values (or throw) instead of defaulting to `female`.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:61,100,348 — `debugPrint` logs production/character/voice IDs in plaintext; if production IDs or voice account IDs are user-identifying this leaks them to console logs — consequence: sensitive identifiers in device logs — fix: redact or drop identifiers from log lines.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:374 — `resolveVoice` hardcodes fallback voice `'af_heart'` when the pool is empty — consequence: an empty preset pool silently assigns a fixed voice regardless of locale/gender instead of surfacing the misconfiguration — fix: throw or return a sentinel the caller must handle rather than a magic voice ID.
- [low] lib/data/services/voice_config_service.dart:184 — `pool` selection for non-male gender falls back to `maleVoices` when `femaleVoices` is empty (`femaleVoices.isNotEmpty ? femaleVoices : maleVoices`) — direction of failure: a misconfigured preset resolves to the riskier (wrong-gender) pool silently — fix: return empty and skip assignment (the `pool.isEmpty` guard at 186 already handles that) instead of crossing pools.

- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:31-48 — chunk-walk loop can advance `offset` past the data chunk without ever setting `dataStart`/`dataLength` when a chunk id is neither `fmt ` nor `data` and the loop's `break` only fires on `data` — consequence: a valid PCM WAV whose `data` chunk appears after a chunk the loop skips (e.g. a `LIST`/`CUE ` chunk) is parsed as having no data chunk, `_parse` returns null at line 49, and `prepend` silently returns `source` unchanged (line 72) so the silence-prepend workaround never applies and the first-line syllable loss recurs with no error surfaced — smallest safe fix: continue scanning all chunks (don't `break` early) and record the first `data` chunk's start/size regardless of chunk order, or at minimum log/return a distinguishable failure instead of the silent `source` fallback.

- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:53-55 — when the file's `dataLength` field is larger than the bytes actually present, the code silently clamps to `available` — consequence: a truncated-but-header-optimistic file gets padded and rewritten with a data-chunk size field (line 93-94) set to `dataLength + padBytes` where `dataLength` was the clamped value, so the written file's chunk size no longer matches the true audio length and downstream players may read past the buffer or misreport duration — smallest safe fix: treat `dataLength > available` as a corrupt file and return null (fail closed) rather than silently clamping and rewriting.

- [medium] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:98-100 — the outer `catch (_)` swallows every exception (I/O errors, parse errors, write failures) and returns `source` — consequence: any failure (disk full, permission denied, malformed file) is indistinguishable from "not a WAV we understand"; the caller cannot tell whether the prepend workaround was applied or skipped, and the rehearsal first-line bug silently recurs — smallest safe fix: catch specific exception types (or at least rethrow non-parse errors) and surface a log/flag so callers can distinguish "unsupported format" from "operation failed".

- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:76-79 — `padBytes` is computed from `byteRate * silence.inMilliseconds / 1000` rounded, then floored to a multiple of `frameSize` — consequence: for unusual `byteRate`/`frameSize` combos the rounding can produce `padBytes` that is a nonzero multiple of `frameSize` but still not exactly the requested silence duration (off by up to one frame), a minor audio-timing drift; also if `frameSize` were ever 0 the modulo would throw (guarded only by the `frame > 0 ? frame : 2` fallback at line 114, so currently safe) — smallest safe fix: document the ±1-frame tolerance or compute pad length in frames directly (`padFrames = (byteRate/frameSize * ms/1000).round()`).

- [low] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:104-118 — `_frameSize` re-walks the entire chunk list from offset 12 on every call and, if no `fmt ` chunk is found, returns the hardcoded fallback 2 — consequence: for a WAV whose `fmt ` chunk is not the first chunk after `WAVE` (legal per RIFF spec), the walk still finds it (fine), but if the fmt chunk is absent entirely the fallback assumes 16-bit mono, which can mis-pad stereo/24-bit files by shifting every sample — smallest safe fix: reuse the parsed `_WavInfo` (store channels/bits there during `_parse`) instead of a second walk with a blind fallback.

- [info] lib/data/services/wav_silence.dart:70 — `File(source).readAsBytes()` loads the entire WAV into memory with no size guard — consequence: a very large file (multi-hundred-MB TTS output) is fully resident plus copied again via `sublist`/`BytesBuilder` at lines 82-88, risking OOM on low-memory devices during rehearsal — smallest safe fix: stream the copy (read header, then stream data chunk) or enforce a max-size guard before reading.

- [medium] integration_test/android_kokoro_rtf_test.dart:28-35 — 6-minute sideload wait loop has no early-exit on permanent absence and no failure report — if the pack never lands the test silently burns the full 6 minutes then proceeds to synthesize with missing model files, failing later with an opaque ONNX error instead of a clear "sideload missing" message — break out of the loop with an explicit fail/`expect` after the deadline (or check `sw.elapsed` and `fail('models never arrived')`).
- [medium] integration_test/android_kokoro_rtf_test.dart:29-31 — readiness probe checks `fp32/model.onnx` and `fp16/model.fp16.onnx` but the loop's break condition also requires `fp16/READY`, while the fp32 dir has no READY sentinel — a partially-copied fp32 tree (model present, voices/tokens still streaming) passes the wait and the timed run reads a truncated `voices.bin`/`tokens.txt`, producing garbage audio or a crash mid-benchmark — require a READY marker (or full file-size check) for both fp32 and fp16 dirs before breaking.
- [low] integration_test/android_kokoro_rtf_test.dart:60,62 — `tts.generate(...)` results are discarded for the warm-up and the timed run's `audio` is only used for `samples.length` — if `generate` returns a null/empty result on engine failure the rtf denominator `dur` is 0 and the printed rtf is `∞`/NaN, silently corrupting the benchmark output — assert `audio.samples` non-empty (and `sampleRate > 0`) before computing `dur`.
- [low] integration_test/android_kokoro_rtf_test.dart:69 — `tts.free()` is not called when the enclosing `for` loop throws (e.g. `generate` throws on a truncated model), leaking the native ONNX session for the rest of the process — wrap the per-iteration body in try/finally so `tts.free()` always runs.
- [medium] integration_test/android_kokoro_service_test.dart:24-27 — same unbounded sideload wait shape as the rtf test: 6-minute polling loop with no deadline failure, so a missing pack costs 6 minutes then a misleading `expect(..., isKokoroReady)` failure — add an explicit timeout failure after the loop.
- [medium] integration_test/android_kokoro_service_test.dart:55-59 — `Future.wait` on two `svc.synthesize` futures: if either synthesis throws, `Future.wait` rethrows and the other future's error is silently swallowed (unhandled async error), and the test's `expect(both...)` never runs — use `Future.wait(..., eagerError: false)` semantics deliberately or `await both[0]; await both[1];` individually so each error surfaces; also `both.whereType<String>()` hides a null result as a count mismatch rather than naming which voice failed.
- [medium] integration_test/android_kokoro_service_test.dart:65-76 — the stale/urgent race asserts `stale` completes to `null` after only a 400 ms head start; if the engine finishes the long line faster than the urgent request on a fast device, `stale` is a non-null path and the test fails spuriously (flaky), and if `synthesize` throws instead of returning null the `await stale` rethrows with no context — widen the head start or assert `stale` is null-or-earlier-completed with a clear reason, and wrap in `onError` to surface engine exceptions.
- [low] integration_test/android_kokoro_service_test.dart:44-46 — `File(path!)` then `existsSync`/`lengthSync` on a path returned by the service: if `synthesize` returns a path in a temp dir the service later cleans up, the sync file checks race the service's cleanup — verify the service contract (path lifetime) or copy bytes before asserting length.
- [low] integration_test/android_kokoro_service_test.dart:80 — `svc.stop()` is awaited but if any earlier `expect` throws the test body exits before `stop()`, leaving the engine isolate running for subsequent tests in the same process — put `stop()` in a `tearDown`/finally so the engine is always shut down.

- [medium] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:171-183 — `_fetch` has no response-status or timeout handling — a 4xx/5xx or stalled Hugging Face transfer silently yields an empty/partial WAV that then fails downstream with a confusing resample/decode error instead of a clear fetch failure — check `res.statusCode` and set `client.connectionTimeout`/response timeout before reading the body.
- [medium] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:186-216 — `_resampleTo16k` parses the WAV header without validating `dataStart`/`dataLen` (both stay -1/0 when the chunk walk never finds `data`) — a truncated or non-PCM file produces `outN = 0` and a silent empty buffer, and the recognizer test then reports "no transcript" rather than "bad fixture" — assert `dataStart > 0 && dataLen > 0` (and `rate > 0`) before resampling.
- [low] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:200 — chunk-walk advances `i += 8 + sz + (sz & 1)` even when `sz` is garbage (e.g. a non-WAV file whose bytes happen to parse) — can loop past `wav.length` only because the guard `i + 8 <= wav.length` re-checks each iteration, but a huge `sz` skips the `data` chunk entirely and the function returns an empty buffer with no error — same fix as above: validate the parsed header before use.
- [low] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:94-97 — PCM feeding loop slices `pcm16k` in 3200-byte chunks but never checks `pcm16k` is non-empty — if the resample produced zero samples (see above), `feedPcm` is never called and `endUtterance` settles on an empty transcript, masking the fixture failure as a recognizer failure — guard the loop with an `expect(pcm16k.isNotEmpty, true)`.
- [info] integration_test/android_live_matching_test.dart:25-26 — test fixture URL is a hard-coded `resolve/main` Hugging Face link for a dated model snapshot (`2025-08-06`) — the wav can disappear when the snapshot is pruned, breaking the test with a network error rather than a local fixture — consider bundling the wav in `test_wavs/` or pinning to a revision hash.
- [low] integration_test/android_paddle_ocr_test.dart:29-32 — `candidates.firstWhere(..., orElse: () => '')` resolves the missing-fixture case to an empty string, then the next `expect` is the only guard — if the expect is ever relaxed or the test is copied, `PaddleOcrChannel.ocrPdf('')` would be invoked with a bogus path — fail fast with a dedicated exception instead of a sentinel empty path.

- [medium] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:100-109 — sideload wait loop can exit on timeout without the packs being ready, then the two `expect`s at 106/108 fail with a generic reason instead of distinguishing "still downloading" from "download failed" — a hung download burns the full 6-minute budget before reporting, and the loop's `break` condition (`kokoro && asr`) is checked before the expects, so a pack that never becomes ready is only detected after the whole window — smallest safe fix: add an explicit failure branch inside the loop (e.g. `if (kokoro && asr) break;` plus a `fail('packs not ready after ${sw.elapsed}')` when the deadline is hit) so the test fails fast with a clear reason.
- [medium] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:138 — `File(path!).lengthSync()` assumes a 44-byte WAV header and 24 kHz 16-bit mono to derive playback duration; if the service ever returns a different format/rate the computed `durMs` is wrong and the prefetch-overlap timing assertion at 142 silently measures the wrong thing — smallest safe fix: parse the actual fmt chunk (rate/bits/channels) from the WAV header instead of hard-coding 44/2/24000.
- [medium] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:142-143 — `latencies.sublist(1).every((ms) => ms < 1500)` asserts on a hard-coded 1500 ms wall against a real TTS engine on an emulator; a slow-but-correct engine fails the test for performance, not correctness, and the threshold is not derived from any measured baseline — smallest safe fix: assert a relative bound (e.g. prefetched latency < first-line latency) or make the threshold a named constant with a comment tying it to a measured baseline.
- [medium] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:161-163 — `firstMs < allMs ~/ 2` is a timing heuristic on wall-clock synthesis; on a loaded emulator the first chunk can exceed half the total and the test flakes for a reason unrelated to streaming correctness — smallest safe fix: assert `firstMs` is meaningfully below `allMs` (e.g. `< allMs`) or compare against a measured per-chunk budget rather than a fixed fraction.
- [medium] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:207-208 — `gaps.every((g) => g < 2500)` hard-codes a 2500 ms inter-line gap against real playback on an emulator; slow audio I/O fails the test for performance rather than a real pipelining defect — smallest safe fix: derive the bound from the measured audio durations (e.g. gap < audio duration of the next line) or move the constant to a named, documented threshold.
- [low] integration_test/android_rehearsal_harness_test.dart:292-294 — `print` of the transcript substring and recording path leaks the recognizer's raw output and local temp-file path into test logs; harmless in a harness but the same pattern in production code would be a sensitive-data-in-logs defect — smallest safe fix: keep the print but redact/limit the transcript field, or drop the path from the log line.
- [medium] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:20-24 — `String.fromEnvironment('CASTCIRCLE_REPO', defaultValue: '.')` resolves the repo root to `.` when the define is absent, so every model/fixture path (`$_repo/.asr-eval/...`) silently resolves relative to the test's CWD; if the harness is ever run from a different directory the files are "missing" and the failure mode is a confusing `File` exception rather than a clear "set CASTCIRCLE_REPO" message — smallest safe fix: `expect(_repo, isNot('.'), reason: 'set --dart-define=CASTCIRCLE_REPO')` before touching the paths, or resolve the default from `Platform.script`/`Directory.current` with an explicit check.
- [medium] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:55-81 — `_samplesFromWav` walks RIFF chunks but never validates that `dataStart`/`dataLen` stay inside the file, and `dataLen` is taken from the chunk header (attacker/fixture-controlled) rather than `bytes.length - dataStart`; a truncated or malformed fixture yields an out-of-bounds `getInt16` read (RangeError) or a silently wrong sample count — smallest safe fix: clamp `dataLen` to `bytes.length - dataStart` (and `dataStart` to a valid offset) before building `out`.
- [medium] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:86-104 — `_matchRate` implements LCS but the DP recurrence at 98-100 is wrong for the standard LCS: when characters differ it should take `max(prev[j], cur[j-1])` (i.e. `max` of the two neighbors), not the nested ternary `prev[j] > cur[j-1] ? prev[j] : cur[j-1]` evaluated against the *previous* row's `cur` slot; as written the recurrence can drop a legitimate match and under-report the match rate, so a recognizer that got most of the line right can be scored 0% and the test's `total`/mean assertions (167-172) silently pass/fail on a broken metric — smallest safe fix: replace the ternary with `max(prev[j], cur[j - 1])` (or use `math.max`) so the DP is the textbook LCS recurrence.
- [low] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:93 — `if (a.isEmpty || b.first.isEmpty) return 0;` guards `b.first` but not `b.isEmpty`; an empty `got` string produces an empty `b` and `b.first` throws before the guard's second operand is even evaluated in the intended order (Dart evaluates `a.isEmpty` first, so `b.first` is only reached when `a` is non-empty — but if `b` is empty the `||` short-circuits only on `a.isEmpty`, so `b.first` on an empty list throws) — smallest safe fix: `if (a.isEmpty || b.isEmpty || b.first.isEmpty) return 0;`.
- [low] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:137 — `Float32List(4800 + raw.length)..setAll(4800, raw)` hard-codes a 300 ms lead-in (4800 samples at 16 kHz) with no comment tying it to the encoder's actual left-context requirement; if the model's context differs the "mirrors reality" claim in the comment is unverified — smallest safe fix: name the constant (e.g. `_leadInSamples = 4800`) with a comment stating the assumed context length.
- [low] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:143-146 — chunk loop advances `off += 3200` but `end` is computed with `off + 3200 < samples.length ? off + 3200 : samples.length`, so the final partial chunk is fed correctly, yet `samples.sublist(off, end)` on the last iteration can be a zero-length list if `samples.length` is an exact multiple of 3200 and the loop runs one extra iteration — actually the loop condition `off < samples.length` prevents that, so no bug; no finding.
- [low] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:157-158 — trailing-silence flush feeds a fixed 12800-sample (800 ms) zero buffer; the comment says the zipformer needs ~0.8 s, so the constant matches, but it is undocumented as a tunable tied to the model — smallest safe fix: name the constant with a comment referencing the model's right-context requirement.
- [low] integration_test/asr_streaming_macos_test.dart:165-172 — `total += rate` then `total / _lines.length` assumes every entry produced a rate; if `_samplesFromWav` throws on a missing fixture the loop aborts and the mean is never printed, but if a fixture is present yet the recognizer returns empty text, `_matchRate` returns 0 and the mean silently drops without any assertion forcing a minimum match — smallest safe fix: add an `expect(rate, greaterThan(someThreshold))` per line (or per candidate) so a silently bad transcript fails rather than just printing a low percentage.

- [medium] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:41 — WAV payload length assumes a fixed 44-byte header and derives sample count as `(bytes.length - 44) ~/ 2` — real WAVs written by most tools carry extra chunks (LIST/INFO, bext, etc.) or a different header size, so `n` is wrong and every decoded sample is misaligned — consequence: the printed transcript is garbage or the recognizer crashes on malformed input, silently "passing" the one-off verification — fix: parse the RIFF/fmt chunk chain to locate the actual `data` chunk offset and size instead of hard-coding 44.
- [medium] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:40 — `rate` is read from byte offset 24 without validating the fmt chunk position or format tag — if the fixture is not plain PCM16 mono at that offset the recognizer is fed the wrong sample rate and the transcript is meaningless — fix: locate the fmt chunk, assert `wFormatTag == 1` and `bitsPerSample == 16`, then read `nSamplesPerSec` from the parsed chunk.
- [low] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:37 — `File('$_dir/test_wavs/0.wav').readAsBytesSync()` has no existence/size guard — if the fixture is missing or truncated (e.g. partial checkout), `getInt16`/`getUint32` throw `RangeError` deep inside the test with no actionable message — fix: `expect(File(...).existsSync() && File(...).lengthSync() > 44, isTrue, reason: 'fixture missing/truncated: $_dir/test_wavs/0.wav')` before decoding.
- [low] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:48 — a second `acceptWaveform` call feeds a 12800-sample zero buffer as a flush tail without any comment or guard — if the recognizer's endpointing/flush semantics change this silently alters the transcript; also the tail is fed at `rate` (the wav's rate) even though it is a synthetic flush, which is only correct if `rate == 16000` — fix: document the flush-tail intent and feed the tail at the same rate used for the real samples, or use the recognizer's explicit flush API.
- [low] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:52 — result is only `print`ed; the test body contains no assertion on the transcript text, so the test can never fail on a wrong transcription — consequence: a regression in the ASR pipeline or a corrupted fixture still reports green — fix: `expect(recognizer.getResult(stream).text, contains('expected words'))` (or compare against the known fixture words) instead of only printing.
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:36 — `_matchRate` guards `a.isEmpty || b.first.isEmpty` but `b` itself is never checked for emptiness — if `heard` is empty, `words('')` returns `['']` (split of empty string yields one empty element), so `b.first.isEmpty` happens to catch it, but if `expected` is empty while `got` is non-empty the LCS loop still runs on `a.length == 0` and `prev[b.length] / a.length` divides by zero (integer division by zero → `IntegerDivisionByZeroException`) — consequence: a crash instead of a 0% match when the expected line is somehow empty — fix: `if (a.isEmpty || b.isEmpty || b.first.isEmpty) return 0;` (or guard `a.length` before the division).
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:47 — `prev[b.length] / a.length` performs integer division; the match rate is truncated to an integer percentage before the `> 0.8` comparison — e.g. 4/5 words matched yields `0` (int 4/5 = 0), so a near-perfect transcript scores 0 and the test fails spuriously — fix: `return prev[b.length] / a.length` with `double` arithmetic (`prev[b.length] / a.length` on `int`s in Dart already yields `double`... but here both operands are `int` so `/` yields `double`; re-check: Dart's `/` on ints yields double, so this is only a problem if the intent was `~/`) — re-evaluate: Dart `int / int` returns `double`, so no truncation occurs; the real issue is that the LCS `prev`/`cur` arrays are indexed `1..b.length` but `cur[j]` for `j == 0` is never seeded, leaving column 0 as 0 which is correct for LCS — no defect; withdraw this line.
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:43 — the LCS inner branch `prev[j] > cur[j - 1] ? prev[j] : cur[j - 1]` computes `max(prev[j], cur[j-1])` but omits the standard third term `prev[j-1]` when characters mismatch — for the classic LCS recurrence the mismatch case is `max(prev[j-1], prev[j], cur[j-1])`; omitting `prev[j-1]` can undercount matches when the mismatch cell dominates via the diagonal — consequence: `_matchRate` can return a lower score than the true word-level LCS, making the `> 0.8` gate fail spuriously for a correct transcript — fix: use the standard recurrence `cur[j] = max(prev[j-1], max(prev[j], cur[j-1]))` on mismatch.
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:55-57 — `_to16k` linear-interpolates by advancing `src` by `fromRate/16000` per output sample but computes `i0`/`i1` from `src` without clamping `src` itself to `x.length - 1`; when `x.length * fromRate / 16000` is not an integer the last output sample can read `x[i1]` where `i1 == x.length - 1` but `src > x.length - 1`, producing a slightly wrong final sample — consequence: negligible amplitude error at the tail, harmless for ASR — fix: clamp `src` to `x.length - 1` (or accept as benign).
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:102 — the flush tail `Float32List(12800)` is fed at 16000 Hz for every voice; if `audio.sampleRate` is not 16000 the tail duration relative to the resampled audio is inconsistent, but since `_to16k` already resamples to 16 kHz this is consistent — no defect; withdraw.

- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:107 — `stream.free()` is likewise skipped when an `expect` inside the loop throws before it (line 111's `expect` runs after `free`, but line 97's `expect` runs before `stream` is even created, so only line 111 can throw after `stream.free()` — actually line 111 is after `stream.free()` at 107, so the only leak path is line 97 throwing before `stream` exists, which leaks nothing) — no defect; withdraw.
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:96 — `tts.generate(...)` result is used without a null/empty guard beyond the length expect; if the plugin returns a null `audio` on some platforms the test crashes with an unhelpful error — fix: `expect(audio, isNotNull, reason: ...)` before reading `.samples` (low value; optional).
- [low] integration_test/kokoro_pack_smoke_macos_test.dart:27 — the `_voices` map hard-codes speaker IDs (3, 11, 21, 26) that must match the shipped `voices.bin` layout; if the pack's voice table changes order the test silently synthesizes with the wrong voice while still passing — consequence: the smoke test no longer validates the voices it claims to — fix: derive the sid from the pack's voice index file or assert the mapping against a checked-in manifest (test-only, low).
- [info] integration_test/asr_testwav_transcript_macos_test.dart:13-14 — `CASTCIRCLE_REPO` defaults to `'.'` (checkout root); if the test is run from a different working directory the staged fixture paths silently resolve to nonexistent files — consequence: the one-off test fails with a confusing file-not-found rather than a clear staging error — fix: resolve the repo root from the script location or fail fast with an explicit message when `$_dir` does not exist.

- [medium] integration_test/ocr_page_repeat_macos_test.dart:38-47 — `Future.wait` on three concurrent `ocrPage` calls has no error handling; a single failing future rejects the whole `Future.wait` and the test aborts with an opaque stack instead of reporting which page failed — use `Future.wait(..., eagerError: false)` or individual try/catch to surface per-call failures.

- [medium] lib/features/settings/debug_log_screen.dart:75-81 — debug report upload inserts the full log text (which can contain user email, device memory stats, and arbitrary app messages) into a Supabase table with no confirmation and no size/PII scrubbing — any authenticated user tapping "Send to developer" ships potentially sensitive content to a shared backend table; add a confirmation dialog and redact/scrub user-identifying fields before insert.
- [medium] lib/features/settings/debug_log_screen.dart:164 — `throw Exception('Crashlytics test fatal error')` inside a button handler throws synchronously in the widget tree with no enclosing try/catch — the uncaught exception crashes the app in release builds too (not just debug), destroying user state; guard behind a debug-only flag or catch and route to Crashlytics explicitly.
- [medium] lib/features/settings/debug_log_screen.dart:170 — `FirebaseCrashlytics.instance.crash()` is invoked unconditionally from a menu action — this deliberately terminates the process from a UI tap reachable by any user of the debug screen; require an admin/dev-build gate before exposing the action.
- [low] lib/features/settings/debug_log_screen.dart:32-42 — `Timer.periodic` callback calls `setState` via `mounted` check but the timer is created in `initState` and only cancelled in `dispose`; if the widget is disposed while the timer fires between frames the `mounted` guard is correct, but the timer also keeps running during the build phase of a parent that removed this screen — cancel in `deactivate` as well or use `WidgetsBinding`-scheduled refresh to avoid a one-frame leak; low impact.
- [low] lib/features/settings/debug_log_screen.dart:223-232 — `FloatingActionButton` onPressed calls `_log.getMemoryUsage()` and logs the result but never updates `_lastPhysicalMB/_lastAvailableMB` shown in `_buildMemoryBar` until the periodic timer or another rebuild happens — the "Check memory now" button appears to do nothing visually; call `setState` after the await (it does, line 228) but the bar values come from `_log.lastPhysicalMB` captured in `build` before the async result lands, so the displayed numbers lag one rebuild behind; recompute the bar from the awaited `mem` map.
- [low] lib/features/settings/ai_models_screen.dart:36 — `_checkOnnxStatus()` is fired in `initState` without any error handling and its `Future` is not awaited — if `ModelManager.instance.isKokoroReady()` throws, the error is an unhandled async exception with no user-visible path; wrap in try/catch or add `.catchError`.
- [low] lib/features/settings/ai_models_screen.dart:110 — `_deleteOnnxKokoro` awaits `clearCache()` then immediately sets `_onnxReady = false` without verifying the delete succeeded — if `clearCache` throws (disk error), the catch-less async gap crashes the zone and the UI still shows the model as deleted on next rebuild because `_onnxReady` was set before the await completed in the caller's `mounted` block ordering; wrap in try/catch and only flip `_onnxReady` on success.
- [low] lib/features/settings/ai_models_screen.dart:333-335 — group delete loops `await _service.delete(m.id)` sequentially with no error handling — a failure on the second file leaves the first deleted and the rest stale, and the exception escapes the `onSelected` callback unawaited; collect failures and surface one summary toast.

Now finalize findings:

A. kokoro_debug_screen.dart:48 — `_loadDebugInfo` awaits `_tts.getDebugInfo()` outside the try/catch; called fire-and-forget from initState (line 33) with no error path; if TtsService.getDebugInfo throws (assumption: it may throw before `init()`), `_loading` stays true forever (infinite spinner) and the async error is unhandled. Severity: medium (any user opening the debug screen; stuck UI + unhandled async exception). Fix: wrap line 48 in the existing try/catch (or try/catch around the whole body) and set `_loading = false` in a finally.

B. kokoro_debug_screen.dart:94-106 — `_tryInit`/`_tryReload` await `_tts.init()`/`tryLoadKokoro()` with no try/catch (unlike `_speak` at 117-123); on failure the success log lines never appear and no error is logged — user gets no feedback and `_loadDebugInfo` (line 98/105) never runs. Low (debug screen, operator-driven). Fix: wrap in try/catch and `_log('... ERROR: $e')`.

C. kokoro_debug_screen.dart:118 — `setRate(_speed * 0.5)` while the UI reports `Speed: ${_speed}x` (line 258): the applied rate is half the displayed value (slider 2.0x sends 1.0). Assumption: TtsService.setRate expects the same multiplier the UI displays; if it expects a 0–1 normalized rate this is intentional. Low. Fix: pass `_speed` (or display the halved value).

D. kokoro_debug_screen.dart:90 — `_statusLog` prepends every message and is never trimmed; a long debug session grows the string (and the monospace Text) without bound, raising memory and rebuild cost on a screen that stays open. Low. Fix: cap retained lines (e.g. keep last ~200 lines).

E. kokoro_debug_screen.dart:135-359 — oversized build() (~225 lines with deeply nested literals). Low. Fix: extract status/actions/speak/files/log sections into builder methods. Hmm — is this worth it? The checklist lists it. I'll keep it but low.

F. model_download_screen.dart:46-53 — `_checkStatus` awaits `_manager.isKokoroReady()` with no error handling and is fired from initState (line 32) and after `clearCache` (line 254); if it throws (assumption: ModelManager may throw when the models directory is unreadable), the async error is unhandled and `_kokoroReady` stays false. Medium per checklist class (initState async with no error path). Reachability: any user opening the screen. Fix: try/catch inside `_checkStatus` (log or set an error state).

G. model_download_screen.dart:92-264 — oversized build() (~170 lines). Low. Fix: extract the branch cards into helpers.

Write the report.
- [medium] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:292-296 — Web-editor share text embeds the signed-in user's email in a share sheet — any authenticated user's own email is disclosed to whatever app receives the share (clipboard, contacts, other apps); reachability: authenticated user against their own data — smallest safe fix: omit the email from the share text (or show it only in-app, not via Share.share).
- [medium] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:324-359 — `_signOut` awaits `sharedPreferencesProvider` removal and Supabase sign-out, then navigates with `context.go('/auth')` guarded by `context.mounted`, but the widget is a `ConsumerWidget` whose `BuildContext` may be stale after the two awaits — if the screen was disposed during sign-out (e.g. user navigated away), `context.mounted` is false and navigation silently never happens, leaving the user on a screen whose auth state was just cleared — smallest safe fix: capture the `GoRouter`/navigator before the awaits, or use a `mounted`-safe navigation helper (e.g. `router.go` captured pre-await).
- [low] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:69-76 — `_getVersionString` memoizes a `Future` in a top-level mutable `_versionFuture` — if the future completes with an error the memoized future is re-thrown on every rebuild and never retried; the `catch` inside makes this unreachable today, but the memoization pattern is fragile — smallest safe fix: keep the try/catch (already present) and note the memoized-future-rethrow risk, or reset `_versionFuture` on error.
- [low] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:308-317 — `FutureBuilder` on `_getVersionString()` re-executes the future on every rebuild of the widget subtree that owns it — since `_versionFuture` is memoized at module scope this is actually safe here, but if the memoization were removed the builder would re-fire PackageInfo on each rebuild — smallest safe fix: none needed now; keep the memoization invariant documented near the builder.
- [info] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:328 — `sharedPreferencesProvider` is read via `ref.read(...)` inside an async method on a `ConsumerWidget` — reading a provider after the widget may be disposed is safe for `ref.read` on Riverpod 2.x only if the provider is global (it is, a top-level `StateProvider`), so no defect; noted only because the pattern is commonly flagged — smallest safe fix: none.
- [low] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:120-133 — jump-back-lines +/- buttons disable themselves at bounds (1 and 20) via `onPressed: null`, but the slider-style controls (playback speed, line delay, etc.) rely on `Slider` clamping — consistent, no defect — smallest safe fix: none.
- [low] lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart:140-149 — `DropdownButton<JumpBackTrigger>` builds items from `JumpBackTrigger.values` on every rebuild; trivial cost for 4 enum values — smallest safe fix: none.

## Coverage
lib/features/settings/settings_screen.dart — findings: 3
- [medium] tool/analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:130-140 — auth fallback resolves permissive: if signup fails (e.g. email already exists or signup disabled), the code silently retries the same credentials against `/auth/v1/token?grant_type=password`, and if that also fails it returns null only when both tokens are missing — but when signup returns a partial payload without `access_token` yet with a `user.id`, the code proceeds with a null token path guarded only by `at == null` — consequence: an operator running this offline tooling can end up with a client whose `Authorization: Bearer null` header is sent (line 36 builds the header from `cred.accessToken` only after the null check, so this specific path is guarded; the real defect is that the fallback masks signup failure and the operator cannot distinguish "account created" from "existing account hijacked") — smallest safe fix: treat signup success as the only acceptable path and fail loudly if `res['access_token']` is absent rather than retrying the password grant.
- [medium] tool/analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:115 — cleanup delete is fire-and-forget with no `.select()`/result check and errors only printed — consequence: if RLS blocks the delete (same failure mode orphan_sweep.dart:90-104 explicitly warns about), the throwaway `cast_members` row is silently left behind on the production and the script still exits 0, leaving junk data the operator never learns about — smallest safe fix: mirror orphan_sweep's pattern: `.delete().eq('id', memberRowId).select('id')` and warn when the returned list is empty.
- [low] tool/analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:142-157 — `HttpClient` has no `connectionTimeout`/response timeout set — consequence: a hung Supabase endpoint stalls the audit script indefinitely with no error surfaced (the `catch (_)` swallows the exception type, so a timeout is indistinguishable from a network refusal) — smallest safe fix: set `client.connectionTimeout` and wrap `req.close()`/body read in a `timeout()` before decoding.
- [low] tool/orphan_sweep.dart:127-143 — same unbounded `HttpClient` with no timeouts and a catch-all `catch (_) { return null; }` — consequence: one slow auth request silently converts into `auth failed`/exit(1) or, worse, a null that the caller then retries against the password grant, masking the real failure cause — smallest safe fix: add explicit timeouts and rethrow or log the exception instead of swallowing it.
- [medium] tool/orphan_sweep.dart:53 — `catch (_) {}` on the membership insert — consequence: if the insert fails for a reason other than RLS (e.g. the production id is malformed or the table rejects the row), the script proceeds to read with no membership row and reports "read failed" for every production, and the operator has no way to tell "RLS blocked the join" from "the script is broken" — smallest safe fix: print the insert failure (as analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:53 does) before continuing.
- [info] tool/analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:19-20 and tool/orphan_sweep.dart:17-18 — hard-coded Supabase URL and publishable (anon) key in source — these are public anon keys by design, but verify they are not committed if the repo policy treats deploy URLs as secrets; no action beyond confirming git state.
- [info] tool/analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:28 and tool/orphan_sweep.dart:22 — hard-coded throwaway password `'Test-passw0rd!'` shared by both scripts — consequence: any account created by one script can be logged into by the other (and by anyone who reads the repo) since the email is the only per-run secret and the password is constant; combined with the password-grant fallback at analyze_orphaned_recordings.dart:134 / orphan_sweep.dart:119 this lets a later run sign in as a previous run's leftover account — smallest safe fix: generate a per-run random password alongside the random email, and verify neither file is committed with real credentials.

- [low] tool/sim_multi_user.dart:311 — `await b.removeChannel(chan)` is called but `chan` was created via `b.channel(...)` without ever calling `chan.subscribe` completion guarantee; if the realtime socket never connects, `removeChannel` may hang — wrap in a timeout.
- [low] tool/sim_multi_user.dart:43-48 — `exit(2)` on usage error is fine; but `exit(1)` at 62 inside an async `main` after `bad(...)` skips the `finally` cleanup block at 315-341 if credentials partially succeeded — actually `credA`/`credB` null-check happens before `try`, so no leak; no finding.

- [low] tool/parse_stats.dart:43 — `File(positional.first).readAsStringSync()` with no existence/IO error handling — a missing file crashes with an unhandled exception rather than a usage message — wrap in try/catch and print a friendly error.
- [low] tool/parse_stats.dart:31-32 — `--expect` consumes the next arg unconditionally; if `--expect` is the last token, `expectArg` stays empty and the script silently runs without the answer key instead of erroring — validate that a value followed.
- [low] tool/parse_stats.dart:61-62 — `matches` uses bidirectional `contains`, so a short expected name like "ED" would match "ELIZABETH" — precision/recall metric can be inflated by substring false positives — document or tighten to word-boundary matching.

- [low] tool/verify_cloud_recordings.dart:22-24 — `Random()` (non-secure) used to derive the throwaway account email — not a security value (it is a disposable signup identifier, not a secret), but a predictable email could collide with an existing account and silently join someone else's session — use `Random.secure()` or add a timestamp for uniqueness
- [low] tool/verify_cloud_recordings.dart:76 — output filename `rec_${i++}.m4a` uses a post-increment inside a string interpolation inside a loop — if a download fails after a success, the counter still advances only on success, but the `i++` is evaluated during interpolation so a failed download leaves `i` unchanged and the next success reuses the same filename, overwriting a previously written file — hoist `final name = 'rec_${i}.m4a'; i++;` before the write
- [low] tool/verify_cloud_recordings.dart:59-62 — when RLS returns 0 rows the script `exit(1)`s, but the join insert at :40-50 already swallowed its own failure with a print-and-continue — the operator gets "RLS returned 0 rows" with no indication that the join step was the actual failure point — surface the join error code/message in the exit path or rethrow

## Coverage
tool/verify_cloud_recordings.dart — findings: 7
- [medium] lib/app.dart:39-44 — authGatePassedProvider is a StateProvider whose initializer runs once and never re-evaluates when SupabaseService finishes async init — if SupabaseService.instance is not yet initialized at first read (session restore is async), the gate resolves to the PERMISSIVE `false`... wait, actually false is the safe direction for auth, but the redirect at 75-81 then forces every route to '/auth' and the deep-link handler at 238 skips pushing '/join' — the riskier failure is the opposite: if SupabaseService reports isSignedIn true from a stale/cached session without re-validating, the gate is permissive. State the assumption: SupabaseService.instance.isSignedIn is a synchronous flag whose truthiness cannot be verified from inlined code — direction of failure unverifiable here — smallest safe fix: derive the gate from a FutureProvider/AsyncValue of `supabase.restoreSession()` so the redirect waits for session resolution instead of a one-shot synchronous read.
- [medium] lib/app.dart:234-243 — _handlePendingJoin trusts the PendingJoin payload (production id / code) from an unvalidated deep link and immediately writes it into pendingJoinProvider and pushes '/join' without any validation of the link's origin, signature, or parameters — a crafted URL (web/UniversalLinks, custom scheme) can drive the user into joining an attacker-controlled production — smallest safe fix: validate PendingJoin fields (allowed host/scheme, signed invite token) in DeepLinkService before emitting, or re-verify the invite server-side before navigation.
- [medium] lib/app.dart:214-232 — _setupDeepLinks is async work launched from initState with a try/catch only around init(); the listen at 231 and the latestPendingJoin read at 226 have no error path, and if deepLinks.init() throws after partial setup the subscription is never created while dispose() still runs — consequence: cold-start invite silently dropped and a dangling dispose — smallest safe fix: wrap the whole body in try/catch and log, and guard dispose with null-check (already done) plus create the subscription before any await that can throw.
- [low] lib/app.dart:196 — _routerProvider builds a GoRouter eagerly via Provider; GoRouter holds NavigatorObserver state and is recreated if the provider is invalidated/rebuilt (e.g. on authGatePassedProvider writes via ref.read inside redirect at 76) — consequence: router instance churn can reset the navigator stack mid-session — smallest safe fix: keep the router in a Provider that is never recomputed (dependency: build once with a Ref that does not watch authGatePassedProvider; read gate via ref.read inside redirect only, which is already the case — so instead mark the provider `Provider((ref) => _buildRouter(ref))` but ensure nothing watches it; verify no provider refresh) — if confirmed no refresh path exists, downgrade to info.
- [low] lib/app.dart:258-275 — ref.listen callback performs async disk/network work (loadForProduction, launchRecordingSync) inside a synchronous listener with unawaited(); if the widget is disposed mid-listen the captured `ref` is the app-root ref (documented as stable at 255-257) so this is safe for the ref, but `next.id` capture after dispose still triggers a production sync for a dead screen — consequence: background sync for an abandoned production, wasted battery/requests — smallest safe fix: check `mounted`/context before launching sync, or cancel the sync when the production is cleared.
- [info] lib/firebase_options.dart:53,62 — Firebase API keys are embedded in source; these are by-design client identifiers (Firebase web/api keys are public), but verify it is not committed if the repo policy treats firebase_options.dart as generated-and-gitignored — reference file:line only, do not quote values.
- [low] lib/firebase_options.dart:19-24 — kIsWeb branch throws UnsupportedError for web with no firebase_options.web fallback — consequence: any web build crashes at Firebase.initializeApp with a configuration error rather than a graceful message — smallest safe fix: generate web options via FlutterFire CLI or guard web entrypoint.
- [low] lib/app.dart:312-315 — _sendToFirebase calls FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logScreenView without awaiting or error handling; if Firebase is not initialized (firebaseAvailable true but init failed) the future rejection is unhandled — consequence: unhandled async exception / zone error — smallest safe fix: `unawaited(FirebaseAnalytics.instance.logScreenView(...).catchError(...))` or `try/catch` with debug log.
- [low] lib/app.dart:323-327 — didPop logs the previousRoute only when BOTH route and previousRoute are PageRoutes; a pop that removes the last PageRoute back to a non-PageRoute root (e.g. after a dialog/root route) silently skips screen logging — consequence: analytics attribution stuck on the abandoned screen — smallest safe fix: log previousRoute whenever it is a PageRoute regardless of the popped route type.
- [low] lib/app.dart:330-332 — didReplace logs newRoute but never oldRoute; a replace that swaps the visible screen attributes the new screen correctly but the popped/old screen's view count is lost — consequence: minor analytics drift — smallest safe fix: also log oldRoute when it is a PageRoute (or accept by design; document).
- [low] lib/app.dart:47-68 — _screenNames covers only the 20 routes declared at 82-193; any route added later without a map entry falls back to logging the raw path (337), which is fine, but '/welcome' is declared as a route (93-97) yet missing from _screenNames — consequence: analytics screen name for '/welcome' is the raw path instead of a human-readable name — smallest safe fix: add `'/welcome': 'Welcome'` to _screenNames.
- [low] lib/app.dart:75-81 — redirect reads authGatePassedProvider via ref.read inside the redirect callback; GoRouter redirect may run before the first build of CastCircleApp, i.e. before any widget has initialized the provider's StateController — Riverpod StateProvider initializers do run lazily on first read, so this is safe, but the redirect also never re-runs when the gate flips from false→true after async session restore — consequence: user stuck on '/auth' even after sign-in completes if the gate was read pre-init — smallest safe fix: after sign-in/skip, explicitly `ref.read(_routerProvider).go('/')` (the auth screen presumably does this; assumption: AuthScreen handles post-sign-in navigation — cannot verify from inlined code) or make the gate an AsyncValue and `refresh` the router's redirect via `router.go(...)`.
